     | Its premium's volatility | 0.4 | 0 to 3, by 0.1 | `index_volatility`, in ticks a root unit and a unit of scale; at 0 the order flow's own impact still makes a premium of about 1.2 ticks a unit of scale |
     | Deals | off | on or off | `FactorValues(deals=Deals(...))` with the five settings below, a `cool` of 100 and `within` |
     | Deals a 1,000 units | 4 | 0.5 to 10, by 0.5 | `rate` is this over 1,000, over the whole market (the library's default is 0.002) |
     | Premium (ticks) | 60 | 5 to 200, by 5 | `premium`: what the offer is worth over the target at the announcement |
     | Length | 500 | 50 to 2,000, by 50 | `length`: units of time from the announcement to the completion |
     | Priced to break (%) | 40 | 0 to 95, by 5 | `priced`: the chance the market gives to a break, over 100 |
     | Break in truth (%) | 20 | 0 to 95, by 5 | `breaks`: the chance a deal does break, over 100 |
     | Factor trend (sd) | 0 | 0 to 0.05, by 0.005 | `trend`, on every factor but the first, with a half-life of 500 |

     `cool` (100), `within` (True) and the trend's half-life (500) are not on the form (`DEAL_COOL`, `DEAL_WITHIN`, `TREND_HALF_LIFE`), and priced and breaks stop at 95 so that a form cannot make a `Deals` the library refuses. The view's defaults price a break at 40% and have one in 5, which gives the deals trade an edge of 12 ticks a lot pair at fair values (0.8 times 24 less 0.2 times 36), where the library's own deals, `priced` equal to `breaks`, have none: priced above in-truth is a risk premium, and the form's hints say so. **The trend is on every factor but the first**, `(0, s, ..., s)`: the floor pushes the first factor, on which every book must load positively, and in the sectors market a dollar-neutral trader cannot hold the market factor anyway. A mixed market with one factor and a trend is refused at the trend ("needs two factors or more"). At the view's level of 1,000 a long run at a trend above about 0.04 (0.02 in a mixed market) can reach the floor, which pushes the first factor and says so in the status line's amber chip: those are the largest trends that never reached it in 64 windows of 30,500 units on seeds 1 to 8 (not tried on others), and at 0.05 over 30,000 units on seeds 1 to 8 it was reached in 0 of 8 runs in the sectors market and 1 of 8 in the mixed.
   - **IDX, a thirteenth book.** The index is the last book, named `IDX`, in a group of its own (not a sector; in a mixed market, group 1 after the one there is). The default 3 sectors of 4 books is then 13 books, and 4 of 4 is 17. Everything that counts the books counts it: the names, the groups, the mids in each sample, the books panel's rows, the board's chips, the status line ("13 books, 3 factors, 10 relations": the premium is one more relation), What the tests see (Engle-Granger over 78 pairs, 18 truly cointegrated and none with IDX), the fit's budget (`MAX_FIT_BOOK_UNITS` over one more book) and `too_few_books`. Johansen takes 12 at most, and the view says so: What the tests see says "Johansen's test takes 12 books at most, and there are 13" in place of a rank (the status template printed "None" for it, found by the audit), a Baskets trader of all the books by Johansen is refused past 12 (naming baskets of a few books or Box-Tiao as the way out) and so are new settings of 13 books or more while one is on the board. The form's *Books* (3 to 12) set the books the index is made of, with IDX on top. **On the chart** the index line is IDX's mid over the number of the other books, so that it sits among them (its own price is about 12 times a book's), in a color of its own that is none of a sector's, twice as thick, and the legend names it ("IDX, the index over 12"); the board and the book rows show its own price. Exposure on the board and in the samples reads `fair.exposures()`, so that with a deal on a target's is the mix.
   - **The deals line.** With deals on the status panel gains a line (`data-statarb-deals`): how many deals are on, how many have completed and how many broken so far (a tally of `fair.ended` kept incrementally and reset with the market), and a chip for each deal on, "A4 bought by A1 gap +17.0 left 67": the target, the acquirer, the gap in ticks (r times the acquirer's mid less the target's) and the time left (0 in the last half unit). The stream's two new keys are `history()`'s `index` (the index book's place, or None) and `context()`'s `deals` (plain data, or None).
   - **The two kinds**, appended after the eight so that Pairs stays first (ten in all), each through `TRADER_KINDS` as the others, with a hover hint on the hedge switch, the index `size` and the deals `entry`, `limit` and `size`:

     | Kind | Library | Defaults (range) | A fit tries |
     |---|---|---|---|
     | Index arbitrage | `IndexTrader(legs=0)` | window 400 (3 to 2,000), enter beyond z 2 (0.1 to 10), flat within z 0 (0 to 9.9), hedge with the books on, baskets 1 (1 to 10, whole), trade rate 1, no-trade band 0, decide every 5 units, learn from the past on | window 200, 400, 800 and entry 1, 1.5, 2 by default; also on offer the rates and bands of `COST_GRID` |
     | Deals | `DealTrader` | enter at a gap of 0 ticks (0 to 100), deals at once 0, any (0 to 6, whole), lots of the target 5 (0.5 to 50), trade rate 1, no-trade band 0, decide every 5 units; no switch to learn from the past | nothing: no fit |

     **Index arbitrage** has one relation, IDX against a lot of each book (a basket; its chip reads "IDX less each of the 12 books", since `relation_text` lists the books first and would have cut the index off), and the signal pane shows its z-score with ±entry and, above an exit of 0, ±exit as for pairs; unhedged it holds the index alone. The view trades the full replication only (`legs=0`); a sample (`index_basket`) is the library's. One basket is one lot, so a rate of 0.5 with a band of 1 never trades at the default size of 1. Its start says "it trades once it has seen N" where the others say "it chooses" (`NOTHING_TO_CHOOSE`), and `StatArbRunner.fresh` leaves it out of the deferred chooser, since its relation is the market's own and a thread would only make a live run differ from a training run. **Deals** enters at an entry of 0, where the library's is 8, so that every deal is entered at the first decision after its announcement and nothing is selected: a higher entry picks deals whose gap has widened, part of it own parts that revert, and earns a little with nothing priced (a late entry at 1.1 times the opening gap earned +1.1 and +1.6 a lot pair at `priced` equal to `breaks` of 20% and 40%, in the fair values of 2,000 books, seeds 1 to 8; se 0.13 and 0.16). Its board has a row a deal on, which come and go ("A4 bought by A1, in +17.0", or "out"; the header "in 1 of 4 deals on" or "no deal on"), the rows being the deals it saw at its last decision, up to its interval of units ago (they equal the deals on in about 98% of reads; the title says so, and the status line is current). Its signal pane has one line a deal, in ticks, with one dashed level at its entry when that is above 0. A deal keeps a slot for as long as it is on and a slot stays empty for at least one sample between two deals, since Lightweight Charts (4.1.3) joins the points on either side of a gap (the first screenshot showed long diagonals from one deal to the next); `addLine` in `lob-panes.js` has an opt-in `breaks`, which colors the last point before a gap transparent so that the joining segment is not drawn, and only this view's signal lines use it. A start says "Deals 2 has nothing to learn from the past: it trades from its next decision" (it said it had learned from the last 0 points).
   - **A kind is refused in a market that lacks what it trades** (`KIND_TRADES`, `market_lacks`, `StatArbSession._unsuited`): at a start, in a fit's plan, and in new settings that would take the index or the deals from a trader on the board. Nothing is started or changed and no number is taken. A start says the library's own sentence and what to do ("this market has no index book: turn it on in the market's settings and apply"), and so does a fit, which asks it before the grid, since ticking settings would not mend a market (a fit of Index arbitrage with nothing ticked used to answer "Tick at least one setting" first); new settings say "Not applied: Index arbitrage 1 needs an index book, which these settings leave out: remove it, or keep an index book in the market". `start_trader` asks `trader.check(self.market)` itself (through `_unsuited`), since it appends to the market's traders and does not go through `BookMarket.add`; without it an `IndexTrader(legs=3)` in a market with no index traded the last book as if it were the index (144 lots in 900 units, no error), and with `legs=0` raised inside `step()` at its first choice, which ended the view's market.
   - **Why Deals has no fit** (`TraderKind.no_fit`, `NO_FIT_DEALS`). The deals trade earns a little most of the time and loses a lot now and then, when a deal breaks, and a fit ranks settings by their P&L over three runs: since the deals and their breaks are the same for every candidate of a seed (they depend on time alone), candidates differ in which breaking deals they enter, so it would rank them by how few breaks each happened to meet and not by how well they trade. On seeds 1 to 8 at the view's defaults (entry 0, 10,000 units), 282 deals were held, 219 completed at +94 ticks times lots on average, every one positive, and 63 broken at -217, every one negative: 78% of deals earn, and a loss is 2.3 completions. The fit panel says so for this kind ("Deals is not fitted: ...") with no grid and none of its run controls, and a fit request for it is refused with the same reason ("Not fitted: ...", 422), before the grid is parsed, so that "tick at least one setting" never hides it. Whether a fit helps the index kind is not tested either, though it is built and tested to work.
   - **The signal slots.** `MAX_SIGNALS` is 12: the signals a trader shows on the chart, and the chips on the board, as at 42ae383, which in a market of up to 16 books are those of the first 12. With an index book every book keeps its line and chip, up to `SIGNAL_SLOTS` = 17 (4 sectors of 4 and IDX), so that the index, which is last, is not the book cut off; `StatArbSession.signal_cap()` says which, and the page's `data-signals` is 17 in every market (the chart needs a series for each and the page is rendered once; without an index the extra series stay empty). The first build raised `MAX_SIGNALS` to 13 instead, which changed a market of 16 books with all the settings off (13 lines and 25 chips where 42ae383 had 12 and 24), found by the audit and repaired.
   - **A pair with the index, at small sizes.** The choosers of pairs, copula pairs and baskets (by either method) may take IDX as a leg: in a mixed market they pick pairs such as `7 - 0.09 IDX`, and in the sectors market baskets can. Its leg is about a twelfth of a lot for each lot of the book, and it rounds (`rint(size * leg)`) to no lot at a size of a few: at a size of 5, 0.45 rounds to 0, so the board says "7 - 0.09 IDX" while the position is 5 lots of book 7 alone, and several relations on at once do add their IDX legs (a snapshot held IDX +1 against 5 relations). It is the library's rounding and not a defect, and the Pairs summary and the guide say so.
   - **With the new settings off the view's responses are those of 42ae383, but for the new controls and keys.** A form without the new fields gives the old market (a missing setting is off), and `market_from` asks `FactorValues` for an index, deals and a trend only when one is on, so that with all three off it is called as it was (a test records the call argument by argument, and holds that a trend of 0 is not passed; it was caught only there, since a zero trend changes no numbers). Checked three ways. `tests/test_stage4_identity.py` holds the view's default market equal to the library's at 42ae383 (3S.9). Two trees, a `git archive` of 42ae383's `app` and `lob` and the working tree, were driven through `TestClient` with the same requests, 146 responses recorded (the page, the first events, Apply with the defaults, each of the eight kinds started and run for 1,500 units, What the tests see, stop, resume and remove, and ten refusals; Baskets with the old defaults in both): after the whitespace was made to match (`{%-` in the new blocks of `statarb_status.html` and `statarb_board.html`, and in the fit panels) 115 of the 136 that can be compared (the other 10 are each stream's first event, which carries an instance ID) are byte for byte equal, 20 are equal once the nine settings and the `index` key are dropped from the JSON, and one, the page, differs only by its stamps, the guide's sentence, the new controls and kinds, `data-signals` (17 where it was 12) and the Baskets defaults of 2026-11-11. The first pass of that had 93 and 20, and 22 whitespace-only differences and the 16-book market above. And a test holds the panels and the Pairs page to what they were, to the space (the Pairs page gains its `data-pairs-fit-controls` wrapper only for a view with a kind that has no fit, which only this one has). `guide.html` says up to 16 books (12 when their loadings are in no order), names the three settings and the two kinds, and says where the Stat-arb index sentence's rounding bites.
12. **What notebook 16 finds** (`notebooks/16_what_notebook_15_left_open.ipynb`, 2026-11-14; no change to the library: every market and trader in it is notebook 15's as it stands). Notebook 15 ended with three things it could not settle, and notebook 16 takes each up. **Does reading the news pay a pairs trader on its own pairs, where there is a risk premium?** In notebook 15 the reader (out of a deal's target for the deal's life) led the blind trader at a true chance of a break of 0.20, against a price of 0.40, by +429 (se 256) on eight seeds: a lead, short of two standard errors, and not a finding (3S.10). **Why are the pairs chosen in the market with a risk premium worth less?** The pairs that a trader chose in the market of 0.40 did 446 to 635 better in the market of 0.20 than the pairs that market's own mids gave it (7 seeds of 8; after the run, one comparison seen three times), and nothing explained it. **Would sector index books let a residual hedge with one leg, more cheaply?** 3S.9's index is of all twelve books, and sector indexes were left out. The introduction was written before the run as "settles two and closes the third"; after it, the first is settled, the second is answered on a route with no order books and not in the market, where the 24 new seeds did not show the gap and its size stays open, and the third is closed at this market's price of an index lot. P&L is in ticks times lots after costs over 20,000 units of time, unless a line says "before costs" (never an edge) or "fair values"; every figure below is as the conclusions cell prints it, with its standard error over the seeds (se), t and the seeds positive, and a plain difference or ratio of two printed figures says so. The findings, in the notebook's order:
   - **Seeds, and what was fixed before the run.** Four groups, and nothing was chosen on any of them: everything explored (the settings, the questions, the designs of the tests) was explored on seeds 1 to 100, and seeds 1 to 8, where the settings were chosen, are never pooled with these. `FIRST`, seeds 101 to 108 (8), is notebook 15's eight: section 1 reproduces notebook 15's rows on them and asserts that they equal what notebook 15 printed, to the last digit (all 6 lines of 1a), and they are never tested again, because section 1's hypothesis came from them (and from seeds 1 to 8). `NEW`, seeds 121 to 144 (24), were fixed before the notebook was written and no market run of notebook 15 used them: section 1's test is on these alone. `PAPER`, seeds 201 to 400 (200), are for section 2's route with no order books, which takes a few seconds a seed. `FEW`, seeds 101 to 108 again, are section 3's. The test was written into its cell before the run, with the texts of its four outcomes (more than two standard errors; between one and two; under one; negative) in the cell before it (`cells/12_news_b_0_outcomes.md`, md5 `b9caa7b54367261ae2f62a1154768ae7`, byte-identical in the finished notebook).
   - **Section 1: the test is met, and the first of its four outcomes holds.** The reader out for a deal's life less the blind trader at 0.20, each on its own market's pairs, on the 24 seeds of `NEW`, paired by seed, one-sided, a lead more than 2 standard errors above 0: +576 (se 116, t 4.98, 19 of 24 seeds positive), t 4.98 on 23 degrees of freedom, a one-sided p under 0.0001; the sign test, shown as robustness and not as the test, has p 0.0033; the cell's line reads "MET". `FIRST`'s lead, notebook 15's own, reproduced and not tested again, is +429 (se 256, t 1.68, 5 of 8), and the lead on the new seeds is 147 above it (a plain difference). The test puts the lead far from 0 in units of its own standard error and does not show its size: t 4.98 is not a size, and a lead that has cleared a bar is on average larger than the true one. It is one premium of one simulated market, with two true chances and no line through them.
   - **The secondaries of section 1, in their declared order (none a test, and none changes which outcome holds).** (1) The luck-adjusted lead is +553 (se 124, t 4.46, 18 of 24 seeds positive). It was expected to cut the lead's variance, as it did on seeds 1 to 8 (0.66 of it, a correlation of -0.58 with the luck), and it did not: its variance is 1.15 of the plain lead's, the correlation is -0.21, and the standard error is 124 against 116, so it does not sharpen the estimate and is kept as it was declared. (2) Where the lead is made: the targets' books while the deals are on carry +829 (se 134, t 6.19, 21 of 24) and everywhere else -254 (se 126, t -2.02, 10 of 24); the bet's arithmetic is -740 a run (61.7 lots net short at 12.0 ticks a lot, 38.9 deals counted), so the targets' books less the bet avoided are +89, 0.7 of the books part's standard error (a plain ratio). (3) The cost of reading at 0.40, where the bet is fair, is -454 (se 153, t -2.97, 8 of 24), below 0 as expected, and -419 (se 105, t -4.00, 5 of 24) luck-adjusted. (4) On one set of pairs, the pairs of 0.40 held in the market of 0.20, the reader leads by +530 (se 103, t 5.15, 22 of 24); the difference of leads across 0.20 and 0.40 on that set is +984 (se 125, t 7.88, 24 of 24 seeds positive), and +1,030 (se 165, t 6.25, 22 of 24) on each market's own pairs, where the sets differ (the two markets chose 5.6 of the eight pairs in common on average, by seed 2 to 8). (5) The default reader less the blind trader is +258 (se 156, t 1.65, 15 of 24), and less the reader out for the deal's life -317 (se 109, t -2.92, 7 of 24), with a higher `risk_adjusted_long` (+0.006, se 0.001): it gives up P&L for a lower deviation, and the two are said together. (6) `FIRST` and `NEW` pooled, 32 seeds, +539 (se 106, t 5.08, 24 of 32), an estimate and not the test, since the hypothesis came from 8 of the 32; `NEW` less `FIRST` is +147 (se 281, the two standard errors added in quadrature, not a test).
   - **The bet on the lots held, and the cost of reading** (cells `20_news_d8_*`, added after the first run, on a review of it: a description of these 24 seeds and not a test, with nothing chosen from it). The blind trader at 0.20 is net short of a deal's target by +1.60 lots (se 0.08, t 19.89, 24 of 24 seeds positive) at decision 2 after the announcement, +1.68 (se 0.07) at 10, +1.96 (se 0.08) at 50 and +2.02 (se 0.07) at 75, and +1.89 (se 0.07, 24 of 24) over the deal's life weighted by the deal part (pooled over 927, 908, 826 and 782 deals, 1.59, 1.68, 1.95 and 2.01, and 1.88 weighted, 1.18 times the 1.59 on the same 927 deals), so the printed arithmetic of -740 understates the bet: the expected bet with the lots held is -886 (se 39, 24 of 24 seeds below 0) on its own pairs and -942 (se 33, 24 of 24) on the pairs of 0.40, 1.20 and 1.17 times the arithmetic of -740 and -807. The deal part that it realizes, at fair values before the spread paid, is -911 (se 68, 0 of 24 seeds positive) at 0.20 on its own pairs, -891 (se 85, 1 of 24) on the pairs of 0.40, and +104 (se 128, 12 of 24) at 0.40, where the bet is fair. The cost of reading, the reader's lead less the deal part it avoids, is -335 (se 103, t -3.25, 6 of 24 seeds positive) at 0.20 on the own pairs, -361 (se 89, 5 of 24) at 0.20 and -350 (se 88, 4 of 24) at 0.40 on the pairs of 0.40, not shown to differ between the two chances (their difference is -11, se 71, 11 of 24). On one set of pairs the shift of the lead from 0.40 to 0.20, +984 (se 125, 24 of 24), is +995 (se 123, 23 of 24) of difference in the deal part avoided, with -11 (se 71, 11 of 24) left.
   - **What the lead is, as the notebook says it.** By definition the lead on the own pairs is the deal part that the blind trader loses in the targets of deals and the reader avoids, +911 at 0.20 (the expected bet being 886), less a cost of reading of -335 (a plain sum: 911 less 335 is 576). That sum holds by definition, on any seeds; what the cell shows is that +911 is about the expected bet and that what is left does not move with the chance of a break (-11, se 71). **How much of the deal part is the bet is not measured:** a target tracks its acquirer, so the deal part holds some ordinary reversion, and where the bet is 0 a set of the route carries +117 (se 47) and +126 (se 51) a run in the paths of 0.40 on `PAPER` (the premise review's "about 100") and +161 (se 131) and +232 (se 153) on `NEW`; if the reader gives that up too, the deal part avoided is net of it, the bet avoided is larger than +911 and the cost of what reading gives up is larger than -335, by about that much. The -254 everywhere else is not the cost of reading. So reading the news pays here by staying out of an overpriced bet; the notebook does not show that the news says anything about the pairs, that reading pays at another premium, or that it pays in a real market.
   - **Section 2: the route shows notebook 15's comparison on 200 seeds, and on the 24 new seeds neither the market nor the route shows it.** The route has no order books and no costs (fair values, before costs, 200 seeds of `PAPER`). Along the true chance at which a set is chosen, 0, 0.2, 0.4 and 0.6, the completions in the formation fall (18.5, se 0.3; 14.7, se 0.3; 11.0, se 0.2; 7.3, se 0.2), the pairs across sectors among the eight fall (2.15, se 0.11; 1.66, se 0.10; 1.22, se 0.09; 0.60, se 0.07), by 0.51 (se 0.04) a step of 0.2, and the worth of a set in the paths of 0.40 rises (+3,803, se 97; +4,055, se 96; +4,479, se 92; +5,029, se 87), by +410 (se 34) a step of 0.2; the steps are +252 (se 67, 120 of 200 seeds positive), +424 (se 72, 133 of 200) and +550 (se 75, 138 of 200). The set of 0.40 less the set of 0.20 is +424 (se 72, t 5.87, 133 of 200) in the paths of 0.40 and +442 (se 70, t 6.36, 134 of 200) in the paths of 0.20, where notebook 15 made its comparison: of a similar size to its +446 (se 143) in the market, which was after costs on eight seeds, and not that figure again. Its home effect is -18 (se 31, t -0.58, 99 of 200): a set is not shown to be worth more in the market it was chosen in. A pair across sectors makes 401 less than one within a sector (across less within, -401, se 30, t -13.54, 28 of 169 seeds positive); on the 103 seeds where the two sets have as many, the gap is +271 (se 86, t 3.14, 61 of 103).
   - **The 2 x 2 does not say which half does it, the choice of pairs or the hedge fitted to them.** The gap, the set of 0.40 less the set of 0.20 in the paths of 0.40, is +424 (se 72, 133 of 200 seeds positive) from raw values, +321 (se 49, 139 of 200) with the choice cleaned of the completed deals' steps, +340 (se 64, 131 of 200) with the hedge cleaned and +67 (se 31, t 2.18, 114 of 200) with both cleaned (no pair of the sets chosen from cleaned values is across sectors); the interaction, both less the two alone, is -169 (se 55, t -3.08, 78 of 200). Each single cleaning leaves about three quarters of the gap (plain ratios of 0.76 and 0.80) and the two overlap, so the completed deals' steps do it, through either channel, and no share of the gap belongs to the choice or to the hedge. The sets chosen from cleaned values share 7.16 (se 0.05) of the eight pairs across the two true chances against 5.59 (se 0.10) for raw ones. The cleaned sets are controls that no trader can have, so this says where the damage is and not how to avoid it.
   - **Does the market agree: seed by seed, yes; in sign and in size, not shown on these seeds** (2c, the 24 seeds of `NEW`). In A (the market of 0.20, notebook 15's comparison) the market gives +38 (se 177, t 0.21, 11 of 24 seeds positive) and the route on the same seeds -94 (se 186, t -0.50, 12 of 24), with a correlation of +0.74 (+0.49 to +0.88); in B (the market of 0.40, where the bet is fair) the market gives -139 (se 168, t -0.83, 14 of 24) and the route -111 (se 209, t -0.53, 10 of 24), with a correlation of +0.68 (+0.37 to +0.85). The route's figure on `PAPER` is +442 in A and -424 in B, each 2.5 standard errors of the market's, and a two-sided 5% test on 24 seeds would separate a true figure of that size from 0 with probability 0.67 and 0.68, so this is a check of sign and size and not the test. Market less route, +132 (se 130, t 1.01, 13 of 24) in A and -28 (se 157, t -0.18, 10 of 24) in B, is what the books, the costs, the rounding and the difference between the market's sets and the route's add, and it is not "the bet", which the route carries too (the deal part that a set carries in the paths of 0.20 on `PAPER` is -819, se 49, for the set of 0.20 and -787, se 51, for the set of 0.40).
   - **What did not replicate, and how the notebook says it.** (a) The 24 new seeds did not show notebook 15's gap in the market (+38, se 177, against +446), and the route on the same seeds fell to -94 (se 186), 536 below its +442 on `PAPER` and 2.7 combined standard errors (the combined standard error is 199): of 200,000 draws of 24 of `PAPER`'s 200 seeds, 0.18% have a mean as low as -94 and 0.44% are as far from +442 in either direction, and the chance that one of ten such comparisons is as extreme is 4.3% (plain arithmetic, ten being the premise review's rough count and not computed). In the paths of 0.40 the route's figure on `NEW` is +111 (se 209) against `PAPER`'s +424 (se 72), 1.4 combined standard errors, and 5.31% of the draws are as low and 11.00% as far in either direction. The cells that print this (`40_seeds_*`, added after the first run) are a description of the route and not a test: they say how unusual the draw is and not why. (b) No cause was found. `NEW` has more of the completions (+4.17, se 0.42, against +3.73, se 0.14) and its sets of 0.40 shed fewer pairs across sectors (-0.21, se 0.25, against -0.43, se 0.07, not separated), but `PAPER`'s line of the gap on the two gives +369 at `NEW`'s own values where the route gave -94. On `PAPER` the route's gap is the rest, +411 (se 62), and not the deal part, +32 (se 36); on `NEW` the two are -87 (se 124) and -7 (se 164), neither separated from 0; the shortfall from `PAPER` is in the rest in both paths (+411 against -7 and +415 against +40, which is 418 and 375 below, 2.4 and 2.1 combined standard errors) and not in the deal part (plain arithmetic on printed figures). `NEW`'s home effect, +205 (se 75), is 223 above `PAPER`'s -18 (se 31), 2.75 combined standard errors of 81: a second figure of the same seeds that is far from `PAPER`'s, and not an account of the first. So the reading left is an unusual draw of seeds, at the edge of chance, and not a disagreement between market and route; the route on notebook 15's own eight seeds (a diagnostic) gives +491 (se 320), consistent with its market figure at that standard error. (c) The notebook does not say that the market confirms the route's size, that `NEW` refutes the route, or that notebook 15's +446 to +635 replicated in the market: those three figures are one comparison seen three times, and only the blind trader's +446 is 2c's A. (d) The luck adjustment of section 1 did not do what it did on seeds 1 to 8 (above).
   - **Deals or luck** (2d). With only the order flow's luck changed, a formation keeps 7.62 of its eight pairs (se 0.12) at 0.20 and 7.62 (se 0.10) at 0.40; with the true chance changed instead, keeping the seed's own flow, it keeps 5.62 (se 0.30). Kept under other flow less kept across the two true chances is +2.00 (se 0.28, t 7.12, 22 of 24 seeds positive); the number of pairs across sectors changed in 8 of the 48 cells with other flow and differs between the two true chances in 16 of 24 seeds. So the sets of the two markets differ by the deals' outcomes and not by the luck of the order flow: that is about which pairs are chosen, and not what a set is worth.
   - **Section 3: no, at this market's price** (the sectors market, no deals, the 8 seeds of `FEW`; the residual trader of notebooks 12 and 13). It opens a sector from flat 141.8 times a run, and the commonest opening, 83.2% of them, is +4 lots of the book against -1, -1 and -1 of its three mates; the four lots add up to 1 either way in 87.2% and to 0 in 10.9%. Fitted before rounding, in lots of size 5, the book is +3.74 and each mate -1.24 (the four add to +0.02, the largest lot outside the sector is 0.18), the arithmetic's 3.75 and -1.25: 5 lots of the book against 1.25 lots of the sector's index taken apart, which rounded is 4, -1, -1, -1, also what 5 lots of the book against 1 lot of the sector's sum index is when the index is taken apart (asserted in lots and in exposure to the factors). So the two routes hold the same position in these openings, whole lots at size 5 with this fit, and differ only in what they cost; that is not shown at another size or fit. The trader makes +4,067 (se 200, 8 of 8 seeds positive) after costs, and its exact-check line, notebook 13's frontier line, +1,985 (se 160, 8 of 8), equal to the saved line to the last digit: the same runs as notebook 13's, and not a replication. In bare markets at an `index_volatility` of 0.4 the sector index has a half-spread of 2.99 (se 0.04) and a book 1.00; at 5 lots a lot costs 4.00 in the index and 1.25 in a book; at an `index_volatility` of 0 the index's half-spread is 2.92. An index lot is 2.98 book lots, and an index lot less its four books is -1.03 (se 0.02, t -46.97, 0 of 8 seeds positive): cheaper than the four, dearer than one. An entry of 5 lots costs 7.75 by the PCA route and 9.23 by the index route, dearer by +1.48 (se 0.03, t 48.44, 8 of 8 seeds positive), priced walking the book. Replaying the trader's own orders (4,294 lots a run, costing 5,034 ticks to cross) with the cheapest mix of books and a sector index at each decision, the share of the crossing cost saved is 0.01% (se 0.00 points, 2 of 8 seeds with a saving, 0.3 ticks a run, 0.01% of the P&L) at the price that 3b measured, 13.27% (se 0.06 points, 8 of 8 seeds, 667.7 ticks a run, 16.42% of the P&L) at a book's price and 26.52% (se 0.12 points, 8 of 8, 1,334.8 ticks a run, 32.82% of the P&L) at nothing. By the price of an index lot in book lots the share is 26.5% at 0, 13.3% at 1, 6.6% at 1.5 and 0.0% from 2 (the mean saving is under 0.05% from there, no seed has any saving from 4 up, and between 2 and 4 the most that any seed saves is 0.047% of its crossing cost). The reason is in the orders: a sector's four books' lots add up to 0 in 10.7% of them, to 1 either way in 83.7% and to 2 or more in 5.6%, so a residual is neutral within its sector, an index lot only stands in for the mates' lots and brings one more lot of the book with it, and it pays only where it costs less than 2 book lots (3 mates' lots less 1 lot of the book, arithmetic). One index of all twelve books, as the whole hedge, leaves 0.333 of a book's factor variance unhedged, the same for every book, where a sector's own index leaves 0.000 (3d, arithmetic with no seeds). The answer is no, here, and it rests on the price of an index lot, which the library's scale-4 book sets (one book's order flow over four times the reach): that sector indexes cannot help a residual trader anywhere is not shown. These are the savings of one policy, the cheapest mix at each decision, for an index lot that has a price and nothing else, neither the most that an index could save nor a floor.
   - **The three cards** (one recorded run each, on a seed no setting was chosen on; a card is an illustration): section 1's (the reader, seed 121) fills +2,684 and record +2,695, the most it holds 0.64 times the capital; section 2's (a market run, seed 122, capital 150,000) fills -351 and record -350, 0.57 times the capital; section 3's (seed 101, capital 250,000) fills +5,357 and record +5,357, 0.53 times the capital, with the table's figure for the same run +3,426. Their hit rate and profit factor are those of legs, not of deals, pairs or relations.
   - **What stays open.** The lead the notebook names is **a chooser that reads the news and re-bases a target after a completion**: a pairs trader that chooses its pairs after taking a completed deal's step out of the target's prices, which it can see only through the announcement, so that the deals do not damage its formation. It is not built (the premise review of the design, made before the run, named it); whether it would recover any of the gap that section 2 measures is not shown, and section 1's reader reads the news only after choosing its pairs. Also open, as the conclusions list them: how much of the deal part is the bet and how much is ordinary reversion that the reader also gives up; the same measure on seeds other than these 24, since it was made after seeing the first run; and the gap's size in the market beyond notebook 15's eight seeds. The model's limits are the notebook's own list: one simulated market (twelve books in three sectors of four, fair values exogenous and independent of the traders, a trader alone, market orders only, no fees, borrow, financing, queue position or passive orders), a perfectly informative list of deals, two true chances of a break and no line, a route with no books and no costs, cleaned sets that no trader can have, no index book traded by the residual trader, and an index lot at one price. The settings are not independent of seeds 1 to 8, and the notebook makes many paired comparisons and corrects none of them for how many. Four things stay the premise review's and no cell's, and are marked where the notebook uses them: the market's +38 being 1.8 combined standard errors below notebook 15's +446, the noise of the stand-in for the luck, the rough count of ten comparisons behind the 4.3%, and the limit of about 100 a run on how sharply the lead divides into the bet and the cost of reading (of which only the basis, +117 and +126, is printed).
   - **Two runs, and why.** The notebook was run in full twice on 2026-11-14, on 12 workers of the laptop with a per-cell timeout of three hours: the first from 17:36 to 18:01 (25 minutes; 76 cells, 25 of them code) and the second from 20:19 to 20:43 (23.5 minutes; 82 cells, 27 of them code). Readings of the first run came between them: a premise skeptic that remade the printed figures from raw data with a harness of its own (`review/skeptic.md`: no printed figure wrong, five printed sentences in code cells wrong; the test holds; the lead holds with a change, as one account; the one discrepancy is a draw by chance and the claim about "446 to 635" had to be cut back; the 2 x 2 and 2d hold with a change in how they are read; the no for sector indexes holds with a change), a numbers audit of the run (`review/numbers.md`: 252 identity checks, 0 failed) and a check of the finished prose (`review/final_numbers.md`), a judgment reader of the prose (`review/final_judge.md`, with the fix pass `review/fix.md`) and a reproduction of seven figures with |t| above 5 (`review/reproduce.md`: all to the digit). Two things could only be put right by a run: a printed sentence changes only when its cell runs, and what the skeptic had measured (the lots the blind trader holds over a deal's life, the route on the 24 new seeds) was in no cell, and the outputs are the record. So there was a second run. The second run corrected the printed strings of ten lines (the test's p, which printed 0.0000 and now prints "under 0.0001"; the bet, which printed -741 in one cell and -740 in another and now -740 in both; "agree in expectation", which is gone, since the arithmetic counts the lots two decisions after the announcement and the blind trader's net short grows over the deal; "-0", now "-0.5"; "at the touch", gone from the entry lines, which are priced walking the book; the saving curve's last sentence, which put its zero at 4 book lots where the rows print 0.0% from 2; and "and the bet", gone from 2c's market-less-route line) and added the two parts whose heads say "added after the first run, on a review of it": the bet on the lots held and the cost of reading in section 1 (`20_news_d8_*`) and the 24 new seeds against the 200 in section 2 (`40_seeds_*`), both descriptions and not tests, with nothing chosen from them. Every output line of the first run is reprinted verbatim by the second but those ten lines, and no figure changed, because the second run is the same code on the same seeds (the traders now also record, which 1a and 1c (4) assert changes nothing, and the test cell changed only in how it prints the p). So the second run is not a second test or a replication: the test was decided by the first run, on the outcome texts written before it. A second pass read the finished prose against the second run (`review/final_numbers2.md`: no printed figure misquoted; `review/final_judge2.md`), and its 15 findings (5 on numbers, 5 of judgment, 5 minor) were applied through the markdown cells only, in 37 replacements in 11 cell files and then `prose.py sync`, with no code cell, output or seed touched and the outcome texts still byte-identical; a figure that review proposed and that no cell prints was left out on purpose.
   - **What the runs cost.** The second run's last lines print 241 core-minutes in all: 169 in 208 runs in section 1 (1a, the harness on `FIRST`, 25.5; 1b, the test, 39.1; 1c (3), at 0.40, 39.6; 1c (4), the same pairs, 45.1; 1c (5), the default reader, 19.8), 60 in 530 runs in section 2 (the route 10.2, 2c 20.4, 2c's route again for the deal part 11.5, 2d 17.5) and 10 in 32 runs in section 3 (3a 8.3, 3b 1.8), and the three cards, one recorded run each, 2.1. The plan the notebook was built from, a document outside it that no cell prints, put the total at about 240. The first run's timing lines said 260 in all (204 in 208 runs in section 1, 44 in 298 runs in section 2, before the route was walked again on 232 seeds, 10 in section 3 and 2.1 for the cards): the same runs take another time from one run to the next, and no figure depends on it.
   - **Where its tooling and reviews are kept.** `~/.claude/projects/-Users-mengren-Documents-new-projects-limit-order-book-simulation/next_scratch/nb16/` (a copy of `/tmp/lobcheck/next/nb16/`): `cells/`, one file a cell (`NN_name.py` code, `NN_name.md` markdown; the ids are a hash of the file's stem), `build.py` (cells to notebook; it refuses to overwrite one that holds outputs), `prose.py` (after a run: `outputs` writes what every cell printed under `outputs/`, `sync` copies the markdown of `cells/*.md` into the executed notebook cell by cell and keeps every output, refusing if a code cell file differs from what was run, and `status` says what is left), `smoke.py` (a shortened copy on seeds 1 to 6), `NB16_PLAN.md` and `BUILDERS.md` (the plan and the builders' notes), `outputs/` and `outputs_first/` (what every cell printed in each run), `after_run.ipynb` and `after_run2.ipynb` (untouched copies of the executed notebook after each run), `audit/` (the audit of the code before the first run), `review/` (the reviews above and their raw runs), `second/` (the builders' notes of the second run, the outcome texts' md5, and `nb15_pointer.py`, which put the pointers into notebook 15) and `from_nb15/` (notebook 15's cells for the same traders). The design, the map of the code and the premise review of the design, made before anything was built, are in `next_scratch/leads/` (`DESIGN.md`, `map.md`, `review/review.md`). After a run, build.py is never run again (it writes a notebook with no outputs): the prose changes only through `cells/*.md` and `sync`, so that the outputs are the record.
   - **Notebook 15 points here.** Its conclusions and limitations say that notebook 16 takes up the three things it could not settle and what it found, in one sentence each, and the same edit is in its cell files (`stage4_scratch/nb15/cells/91_conclusions.md` and `92_limitations.md`). Only those two markdown cells of notebook 15 changed (`second/nb15_pointer.py` shows that every code cell's source, outputs and execution count, and the cell ids and their order, are as they were).

### T. Microstructure Studies, Footprint, Tape and Triggers (`lob/studies.py`, `app/studies.py`, `app/triggers.py`, `lob/models/informed.py`)
1. **The study band** (Trade view; `components/studies.html`, `static/js/lob-studies.js`). A band across the full width, after the grid of columns and before the Strategies card (never above the grid: the chart column has no slack at 1440x900), with one tile for each of eight studies: its main value, the lines that go with it, and a sparkline of its once-a-unit samples against the mid (the mid in gray, the study in color, each on its own scale, over the last 30 to 600 units of simulation time, 120 by default). The markup is static and no push replaces it, so a form being typed in or a help being read is never reset; the script fills it in place, and only while the Trade view is shown. The **i** on a tile says what the study measures, how it is defined and what to watch for (`STUDY_HELP`); the gear opens its settings, which are the session's and shared by every tab (`POST /api/studies/config`: checked against the spec, a value out of range refused with the field at fault and nothing changed). A change of settings starts that study afresh and clears its samples, since what it gathered under other settings means something else; a study that is switched off costs nothing and starts afresh when switched on. Which tiles show and the sparklines' window are this browser's. The page loads the studies when it opens and again whenever what it holds may be out of date: a push of another version, a gap in the units, and the stream connecting again.
2. **The eight studies** (the defaults, and the range of each setting). Each is a library `Study` (`lob/studies.py`), so a notebook can run it on a `Simulation` through its `observer` (notebook 14, 3U).
   - **OFI**: Cont, Kukanov and Stoikov's (2014) increment of the best quotes and the lots at them before and after each event, summed over the last W units (5; 0.5 to 100), in lots, signed: lots added at the bid or taken from the ask count as buying pressure, and an event that touches an empty side adds nothing.
   - **CVD**: buyer-initiated lots less seller-initiated lots, by the true side, since the last reset or the start of a playback; it counts through seeks and collections. No settings.
   - **Delta of trades**: the same over a window W (10), the side of each fill being `true`, `tick` (above the previous fill's price a buy, below a sell, at it the previous sign; unknown until the first change of price) or `quote` (Lee and Ready 1991: above the mid a buy, below a sell, at it the tick rule; the mid is the once-a-unit sample `quote_lag` units back, 1 by default). For tick and quote the tile shows *coverage*, the share of lots classified, and *accuracy*, the share of those classified as the true side says, which only a simulator can show. **A quote lag of 0 uses the mid just before the event and is exact by construction** (a fill is at the maker's price, on the right side of it): a check, not a result.
   - **VPIN** (Easley, Lopez de Prado and O'Hara 2012): fills fill buckets of V lots in order, a fill that crosses a bucket's end is split, and VPIN is the mean over the last N full buckets (50) of |buys - sells| / V, `None` until N are full. V is set (`volume`) or, at 0, 50 times the median size of the first 200 fills since the start (250 lots for lots of 5; those fills only calibrate it); it is set again when the engine or the model changes, and kept in a recording's start. The side is `true`, `tick`, `mid` (VisualHFT's default form: a fill at or above the mid `quote_lag` units back is a buy) or `bvc` (bulk volume classification at the bucket level: the buy share is Phi(x), x the mid's change over the bucket in units of the standard deviation of the last 100 buckets' changes, so |buys - sells| / V = |2 Phi(x) - 1|, which has mean 1/2 when x is normal). **The sides are on different scales**: the true side on fair-coin flow sits near sqrt(2 / (pi n)), 0.113 at n = 50 fills a bucket, and BVC near 0.5 whatever the flow (Andersen and Bondarenko 2014). VisualHFT's website bands, 0.60 and 0.75, are not carried over (3T.9).
   - **OTR and TTO**: over a window W (10; 1 to 100), the messages (limit and market arrivals, and cancels) per trade, a trade being an arriving order that filled, counted once however many fills it took (the fills an order makes depend on the engine), and the lots traded per lot ordered. Both are market-wide and without the regulator's minus one (MiFID II RTS 9 counts (orders / transactions) - 1 for each member over a session). The scope is `model` by default, the model's own orders only, since a quoting strategy cancels and replaces at every decision and would otherwise set the ratio; `everyone` counts all.
   - **Book imbalance**: (B - A) / (B + A) of the visible depth over the top L levels of each side (10; 1 to 20), from -1 (all ask) to +1 (all bid), a dash while a side is empty. Weights are `equal` (the default; with one level it is the queue imbalance at the touch) or `touch`: a level d ticks from its own side's best quote weighs (1 + d / S)^-2, S the median quoted spread in ticks over the last 100 once-a-unit samples (at least 1; the current spread until there are 20). It is computed when read, not on every event.
   - **Spread**: best ask less best bid in ticks, and its z-score against the mean and the standard deviation (population) of the last 100 once-a-unit samples, from 20 of them, a dash while the spread has not varied; a *spread shock* is marked at a z of 2 or more (0.5 to 10).
   - **Depth depletion and recovery** (resilience): a side's *near-touch depth* is the visible lots at the K prices from its best quote (3; 1 to 20) and its *baseline* the median of its once-a-unit samples over the last 100 units, from 20 on. An episode starts when a take or a cancel leaves the depth at the K prices from the best quote *before that event* below 0.5 of the baseline; it keeps those K prices as its anchor, ends *recovered* when the depth there is back at 0.9 of the baseline (checked after every event on the side, and at each unit sample) and *abandoned* when the side's best quote is more than K ticks from the anchor's or the side is empty. **It is anchored and not measured from the current best quote**, which reads every quote-improving arrival as a loss: in ZI that starts 14 times as many episodes, 99.6% of them from an improving arrival (3U.6). The onset is `below` (the line, as the notebook uses) or `crossing` (the depth must have been at the line before the event). The tile shows the side depleted now and for how long, the median of the last 20 recovery times, the share of episodes abandoned, Large's (2007) restoration (the share of quote-moving takes that a limit order undid within 30 units, and after how long), and the score T_hist / (T_hist + T_now), 1 when nothing is depleted (VisualHFT's recovery sub-score). In the simplified Mike and Farmer model the touch queues hold hundreds of lots and the tile says *no depletions*; in ZI a side is depleted most of the time at the defaults, so *depleted now* is the usual state there and not an alarm (the tile's help says both).
3. **Warming up, live and in a playback.** A study that needs a window shows *warming up* until a full window has passed since its start: OFI, delta and OTR their W units, VPIN until N buckets are full, a baseline, a z-score and a spread scale 20 samples. The same events give the same values live and in a playback (tested on a recorded session: OFI, delta, the OTR counters, the depletion baselines and recoveries and every sample are equal, and a playback from where the board began equals the board it was recorded from); a playback starts cold, from the recording's quotes, and takes VPIN's V from the recording's start.
4. **What the board costs, and where it is off.** In a session's manager, with every study on, an event costs about +3.1 microseconds in ZI under price-time (8.7 bare, 11.8 with the board: +36%), +3.6 in the informed model (+36%), +4.1 in Mike and Farmer (2008) (+51%) and +7.4 in Parlour (+59%; median of five runs of 40,000 steps, process time, on the laptop while it was shared), which is 0.3 to 0.7 ms of a 50 ms frame at 2,000 events a second. So live everything stays on, and an off study costs nothing. A rule that reads OFI adds about 1.3 microseconds an event, and one that reads the book's imbalance about 2.2 (the book is read at most once in 0.1 unit; the noise is about 0.2). The non-interactive paths stay near what they were: **fits and training tapes have no board** (their scratch managers are made without one), **a collection has it off** (only the CVD, the footprint's cells and the tape count, which live in `_record_trade`: 8.57 microseconds a step without the board and 8.66 with it, over 300,000 steps) and starts it over afterwards, and **a seek keeps it off until its cursor is `SEEK_STUDY_LOOKBACK` = 40,000 events short of the target** (the longest default window, VPIN's 50 buckets, is about 25,000 events in ZI) and then starts it over: 3.48 s for a 500,000-event seek without a board and 3.46 s with it, equal within the run-to-run spread (3.25 to 3.60 s). A seek that ends early gives the studies back, and a failed board is not woken by it. A study that raises switches the board off with one notice and the simulation goes on.
5. **The footprint** (the toggle on the candle chart's toolbar; `static/js/lob-footprint.js`). In each bar, at each price, the lots sold into the bid (the left half of the cell) and bought from the ask (the right half), by the aggressor's side, the color deeper the more volume, the numbers where there is room (rows 9 px tall and bars 40 px apart), the busiest price (the point of control) outlined, and the diagonal imbalances marked: buying at a price at least `ratio` times the selling one price below it, and selling at least `ratio` times the buying one price above it, each of at least `minLots` lots (3 and 10 by default, kept in this browser; a column narrower than 12 px has none). It is a Lightweight Charts series primitive on the candle series, drawn in the library's own canvas, so it follows every scroll, zoom and autoscale and lets the pointer through; the candle bodies turn transparent while it is on (not hidden: a hidden series stops the price scale autoscaling). A finished bar's column is drawn once on a canvas of its own and copied while nothing changes, only the bars in view are drawn, and the page keeps the last 2,000 bars. **How it is checked.** The server keeps the cells of each base bar (`BaseBar.cells`, for the last 12,000 bars, about 6 MB) and sends them with the candles of the last 300 buckets once the session's flag is on (`POST /api/chart/footprint`; every tab follows it, through the reload that `/api/candlesticks` gives); a bar's cells are summed over the base bars it holds as its volume is. The tests hold them equal to the trade log at every interval, and in a playback, where fills are stamped before the clock. The page checks every bar that has cells: the buy cells sum to the bar's buy volume and all of them to its volume, and the result is in the chart container's `data-footprint-check` (`ok`, `bad`, or `none` while no bar has cells), never a console error. **Legible at 15 units or more.** A bar of a few units holds only a few fills and the usual few pixels a bar leave no room for numbers: the hint under the toggle says *legible at intervals of about 15 units or more* and turns amber below that. **Widen bars** puts the bars 48 px apart, ending at the newest; switching the footprint on at a narrower spacing (under 24 px) widens the chart once, where the interval reads, and leaves a spacing the user has widened to alone; the zoom slider's range goes to 72 px a bar while it is on, from 32.
6. **The tape** (the Recent Trades card; `components/trades.html`, `static/js/lob-tape.js`). It is a static skeleton now and no longer a pushed panel (`trades` is not in `PANELS`; the manager's `recent_trades` stay). Every fill has a number that is never reused, and the server keeps the last 2,000 as (number, simulation time, price, size, side), stamped with the clock and not the fill's own timestamp (which lags it in a playback after the user acts); the page keeps the last 2,000 and shows about 50 rows, newest first, built once and refilled where they are, so a typed filter, a scroll position or a hovered row survives the pushes. A push says whether its fills replace what the page holds (a new version, or the page fell behind the ring) or come after the last number it holds; a gap in the numbers makes the page load them all again (`GET /api/tape`). **Filters**: a minimum size (lots), a side (all, buys, sells). **Pause** holds the list still while fills go on arriving, as does scrolling away from the top; the button says how many have come since and **Jump to newest** goes back. **The trade rate** is the fills in the last N units of simulation time (1 to 100, 10 by default) over N, from the stored simulation times, whatever the filters. **Tick-rule marks**: with the box ticked, a fill whose tick-rule side (from the price sequence the page holds, as `TradeDelta` takes it) differs from the true side is marked, and a line says *tick rule wrong on x% of n fills* over the rate's window. The download of every fill (the most recent 100,000, CSV or Parquet) is unchanged. Filters, the pause and the settings are this browser's.
7. **Triggers** (the card below the Strategies card; `app/triggers.py`, `static/js/lob-triggers.js`). A rule has a name, up to 4 conditions, how they combine, a cooldown and actions; up to 12 rules. A condition reads one value of one study (12 values: OFI, CVD, delta, VPIN, OTR, TTO, imbalance, spread, its z-score, the resilience score and how long each side has been depleted) and is `above` or `below` a level (true at every check while it holds) or `crosses above` or `crosses below` it (true only at the check where it happens: the previous value was at or below the level and the value now is above it, or the reverse). The conditions are combined **all of** or **any of**, with no mixed precedence; an all-of rule can hold at most one crossing (two studies do not cross at the same event) and is refused otherwise. **A value that is `None`** (a study warming up, a side of the book empty) is never true and is remembered as `None`, so no crossing is read across it. **Actions**: *notice*; *pause the simulation*, at once, in the middle of a batch of steps, by `is_running = False` and a halt flag that ends the batch (never by stopping the run loop's task); *cancel all my orders*; and *flatten my position*, a protected market order for minus the position with what rests of it canceled at once. **The cooldown** is in simulation time, not wall time (a unit is about 30 ms at top speed); a **one-shot** rule disarms itself when it fires, and is the default for a rule that pauses, since a level condition would otherwise pause again as soon as its cooldown passed (the clock stands still while paused). **When it is checked**: after every step of the model (an idle one too, so the studies' windows drain), after every book event of a playback and after each action of the user's, in `_after_event` and never inside `_process`, since an action is itself an order; the flow studies at every check, the book studies (imbalance, spread) at most once in 0.1 unit of simulation time and only when an armed rule uses them. In the manual model nothing steps, so the card says it needs a running model. **A playback**: only notice and pause act, because the recording holds your orders already and a rule that traded again would double them (the notice says *cancel is off in a playback*). **During a seek or a collection** nothing is checked, and what the rules had seen is cleared when it ends (as it is at a reset and a rewind), so they start again at the target; their definitions stay for the session. **Notices** are at most one a wall second for a rule and carry how many firings they stand for; a rule that pauses always says so. The firings (the last 30) are marked on the candle chart, one for each bar, beside your fills, the newest firing of a rule carrying its name. Comparing with the previous *evaluated* value is what a crossing does here; VisualHFT, whose engine this follows, compares with the previous published one, and combines with AND only (3T.9).
8. **The sixth model, ZI with informed traders** (`lob/models/informed.py`; the model menu; notebook 14's section 4). Nothing in the other models knows a price: order flow does not predict it and resting orders earn (3P). This one adds the simplest informed trader to Smith et al.'s zero intelligence, so that toxicity has a ground truth to measure a study against (3U). Its settings are ZI's (mu 6, lam 1, delta 0.03, L 15, lots of 5) and `sigma_v` (0.3 ticks per square root of a unit, 0 to 5), `jump_rate` (0.05 a unit, 0 to 2), `jump_size` (2 ticks, 0 to 50), `kappa` (2 per tick of gap, 0 to 20), `cap` (3 a unit, 0 to 30) and `persistence` (0.5 to 0.99, the chance that an uninformed market order has the side of the last one: 0.5 is ZI's fair coin, and more makes the flow one-sided with nothing behind it, the control for "is it toxic, or only one-sided?"). **The line in the model bar** says what share of the model's market-order lots the informed traders sent and what makers lose to them against the hidden value, counted live from the model's own orders (for example *Informed traders: 15.5% of the model's taker lots (n of m); makers lose 2.19 ticks a lot to them, against the hidden value*; notebook 14 finds a share of 0.155 (se 0.001) and a loss of 2.19 (se 0.03) at the defaults), none for another model and *not recorded* in a playback. **Reveal**, a switch on the chart's toolbar, draws the hidden value as a violet line on the candle chart (*hidden value (revealed)*): it is a session flag and not a parameter (a parameter change rebuilds the model and reseeds it), carried in the candles as `fair`, the value at the last unit of each bar sampled; it is disabled, with its reason, for a model with no hidden value, and says *not recorded* in a playback, which draws nothing: the tape holds orders and whose they were, not the value, and `exit_playback` starts the model with V at the mid. The value is held at or above 20 ticks (the dashboard's mid is 100). The model has no notebook values (`notebook=None`: notebook 05 runs the five others), a run with `kappa` or `cap` 0 and `persistence` 0.5 is ZI's event for event, and a recording of it plays back exactly.
9. **Where the definitions follow VisualHFT, and where its code differs from its website.** VisualHFT (visualhft.com; github.com/visualHFT/VisualHFT, Apache License 2.0, read at commit b36b9a2, 6 November 2026) is a Windows desktop application for live crypto feeds whose study plugins compute on every book update and whose trigger engine acts on their values. Its research notes (R-001, R-002) are the vendor's own. **Definitions that follow its code**: the equal-weight imbalance as the default and the touch weighting w(d) = (1 + d / S)^-2 with d from a level's own side's best quote (S is estimated differently here: a median of once-a-unit samples, where VisualHFT runs a P-squared median over every book update and falls back on the current spread until it has 200); VPIN's equal-volume buckets, a window of 50 buckets, the `mid` classification as its default form and the tick rule as its corrected one; the spread shock at 2 standard deviations (its window is the last 500 spreads); the resilience score T_hist / (T_hist + T_now) (its recovery sub-score); and the trigger engine's operators, cooldown and crossings. **Where its code differs from its website** (which the studies here follow when they are on the website only, and say so): *its OTR counts book changes*, since its code never increments its trade count, so the value is the book changes in the window less one, comparable to neither ratio here; *its VPIN classifies against the mid* (the venue's flag only if the mid is unknown), where the website says the tick rule or exchange flags, and its default bucket is 1 unit of the instrument and not 50 times the median print; *its bands are on its website only* (VPIN's 0.60 and 0.75 are not in the code, and nothing here carries them over: the sides are on different scales, and no cell of notebook 14's section 4 reaches 0.60); its market-resilience bias is a hysteresis latch on the score (arm at 0.30 or below, disarm at 0.50 or above) and the depleted side that failed to recover, not the website's "fast and one-sided by more than 30%"; and its depletion is the whole book's immediacy-weighted depth 3 median absolute deviations below its median, counted only while a large print is anchored, where the website says near-touch depth (the tile follows the website). **Not in its code at all**: OFI, CVD, delta of trades, the footprint, the tape, a trade-to-order ratio, the Book Toxicity Index and Directional Flow Pressure (the last two undisclosed, and not built here); the first six here follow its website and the papers it cites.

### U. Microstructure Signals Against the Truth (notebook 14)
1. **What it asks.** VisualHFT's research scores each of its signals against a baseline the vendor chooses (R-001: the sign of the first new nonzero imbalance after a change of the mid, against the direction of the next change, 55.1% against 50% on Nasdaq ITCH; R-002 and its other notes: pairing an imbalance with a bucket's open time inflated a result, VPIN did not improve a volatility forecast, market resilience did not predict the spread or markouts, order-to-trade ratios did not support an order-pulling trigger). Notebook 14 asks the same of six studies run in the simulator, where the truth is known: the true aggressor of every fill; in the informed model a hidden value V that informed traders trade on, so that toxicity has a ground truth (which orders were informed, what a maker lost against V on each fill); and exact events, every arrival, cancel and fill in order with the quotes before and after, so that nothing is paired by a bucket's time and a count is a count. Each section names its baseline. Section 7 then trades two of the signals, a taker on the imbalance (7a) and a quote joiner that pulls its quotes on VPIN (7b), through the dashboard's own manager and board of studies (the imbalance and VPIN that a trigger sees), each strategy alone in its market and each with quant-plots' scorecard and signal chart.
   - **The markets** are the dashboard's, at its parameters: ZI, Hawkes ZI, MF 2008, Parlour and Informed ZI. The setup cell writes the parameters out and asserts them equal to `MODEL_SPECS`' defaults, so the notebook cannot drift from the dashboard. MF simplified, the dashboard's other model, is left out: its mid does not move (0.0 changes of the mid a run in 4,000 units), so section 1 has nothing to score, and its depth at the three prices nearest the touch is 1515 (se 27) lots against 34 to 220 in the others, so section 5 finds 3 depletions in all. The initial mid is 10,000, as in notebooks 05 and 07 (the dashboard's is 100, at which the informed model's V reaches its floor in some runs of 12,000 units).
   - **The conventions.** Every setting was chosen on seeds 1 to 8, whose runs the notebook does not print; every figure is on seeds 101 and up: 101 to 108 (8 seeds) for the tables that vary one thing and 101 to 112 (12) for the backtests, so no seed that reports chose anything. A figure with a bracket is a mean over seeds with its standard error across them, 7.2 (se 0.1); a lift is in percentage points (pp) and P&L in ticks times lots. Runs are in units of simulation time after a warm-up (a unit holds 60.1 events in ZI, 21.5 in MF 2008 and 13.3 in Parlour): sections 1 to 6 start from an empty book and warm up for 10,000 events, and the backtests start from the dashboard's seeded book and warm up for 40,000. VPIN's buckets are 250 lots (50 median fills) and its window 50 buckets.
   - **What is true by construction** is labeled so where it comes up: the quote rule at lag 0, BVC's VPIN near 0.5, the loss of an informed fill against V, VPIN's inability to lead a jump of V, and the loss of a taker with no information, which pays the spread.
2. **The imbalance and the next move (section 1).** R-001's event design on 4,000 units a run: after each change of the mid, the first event that leaves the mid alone and gives a nonzero imbalance, its sign scored against the direction of the next change, against two baselines, 50% and the reversal rate (predict minus the last change); the lift is the hit rate less the better of the two. **The queue imbalance at the touch beats the reversal baseline by 5.6 to 11.1 pp in every model, and 50% flatters it**: in ZI it hits 71.1% (se 0.1) on 13,014 changes a run against a reversal rate of 63.9% (se 0.1), a lift of 7.2 pp (se 0.1), and 7.4, 5.6, 11.1 and 7.7 pp in Hawkes ZI, MF 2008, Parlour and Informed ZI; against 50% alone the same hit rates read 16.9 to 21.8 pp. **Beyond the touch, equal weights lose to the reversal baseline and touch weights repair that in part**: equal weights at L 3, 5 and 10 lift by -7.5 to +2.1 pp, below 0 in 13 of the 15 cells; touch weights are above 0 at every level (0.5 to 4.3 pp) and below L 1 (1.4, 2.9, 2.2, 3.7 and 0.7 pp at L 10). **The share dropped depends on the setting and on the design**: in ZI 17.3% (se 0.1) of the changes at L 1 and 8.2 to 8.4% beyond, in Parlour 52.8% at L 1; the design that lets the change event's own state give the reading drops 10.2% (se 0.1) in ZI. **R-001's one-tick effect holds for the hit rate and not for the lift**: at one-tick spreads the L 1 imbalance hits 89.1% (se 0.2) in ZI against 64.5% (se 0.1) wider, but the reversal rate is 87.0% (se 0.3) against 55.5% (se 0.1), so the lift is 2.2 pp (se 0.1) at one tick and 9.0 pp (se 0.2) wider: a ceiling, not a weaker signal. **The queue race** (1/2 + (2/pi) arctan |I|, VisualHFT's form at c = 1) fits one-tick spreads and over-predicts the rest: with an amplitude a and c fitted, a is 1.00 at one tick in all five models (c 2.19, 2.19, 0.79, 1.86 and 2.68) and 0.57 to 0.68 over every spread. **The look-ahead trap flips the sign here**: pairing the once-a-unit sample with the change over that same unit, as a join on a bucket's open time does, agrees in sign 24.9% (se 0.2) of the time at L 1, and pairing it with the next unit's change, the causal pairing, 70.9% (se 0.1); R-002 found the same mistake inflated VisualHFT's touch-weighted lead from +1.26 pp to +15.78 pp, so the sign of the bias depends on the book's queues, and a sample must be paired only with changes after its time.
3. **Order flow imbalance: explained or forecast? (section 2).** Cont, Kukanov and Stoikov's fit and a forecast, in intervals of 1, 5, 10 and 50 units over 5,000 units a run, against the baseline of what the last interval's own mid change forecasts. **OFI explains the interval it falls in**: R2 at 10 units 0.322 (se 0.013) in ZI, 0.238 in Hawkes ZI, 0.495 in MF 2008, 0.474 in Parlour and 0.308 in Informed ZI; 0.250 to 0.410 at 1 unit and 0.363 to 0.768 at 50, with a dip at 5 units in ZI (0.322, 0.306, 0.322 and 0.450 at 1, 5, 10 and 50). **Scored against the last change it forecasts almost nothing in ZI, Hawkes ZI and Parlour**: at 10 units OFI's correlation with the next change is -0.23, -0.17, -0.04 and -0.20 in ZI, Hawkes ZI, MF 2008 and Parlour (+0.08 in Informed ZI), but the last change alone forecasts by more (-0.35, -0.32, -0.17, -0.33 and -0.27), so the negative forecast is the mid's own reversal; beyond the last change OFI's partial is -0.01 (se 0.01) in Hawkes ZI, -0.04 (se 0.01) in ZI and +0.04 (se 0.01) in Parlour. **In Informed ZI what it adds is the gap between V and the mid, which the informed orders reveal** (+0.28 (se 0.02); an independent check found it sits in the informed orders' part of OFI), **and in MF 2008 it is, on a reading not tested here, the persistence of its flow** (+0.12 (se 0.01)). **The sign of the forecast depends on the interval**: in Informed ZI -0.20 (se 0.00) at 1 unit and +0.27 (se 0.02) at 50; at 50 units the partial is positive in every model (0.07, 0.09, 0.16, 0.10 and 0.23). VisualHFT lists OFI on its website and not in its code.
4. **Who was the aggressor? (section 3).** Each rule against the true aggressor, which only a simulator has. **The quote rule at lag 0 is right on every fill, by construction** (it reads the mid just before the event): 100.0% in every model, a check and not a result. Against a stale quote the rules have content: at lag 1 the quote rule is right on 90.1, 90.2, 95.4, 86.8 and 87.5% of fills in ZI, Hawkes ZI, MF 2008, Parlour and Informed ZI, and at lag 5 on 77.4, 80.4, 84.8, 72.4 and 77.4%; the tick rule on 92.2, 92.8, 94.7, 90.5 and 90.5%. **Per bucket of 250 lots the tick rule tracks the true buy share and BVC tracks it far worse**: correlations of 0.79, 0.80, 0.95, 0.77 and 0.86 for the tick rule against 0.45, 0.37, 0.71, 0.50 and 0.43 for BVC (mean absolute error 0.028 to 0.043 against 0.200 to 0.233), since BVC reads price changes and not flow. **VPIN's level depends on the rule**: the true side reads 0.113 (se 0.001) in ZI, the value of independent fair-coin sides, sqrt(2 / (pi n)) with n = 50 fills a bucket (by construction in ZI), 0.195 in MF 2008 and 0.170 in Informed ZI; the tick rule reads lower in every model (0.102, 0.099, 0.183, 0.103 and 0.128), and BVC reads 0.504, 0.471, 0.524, 0.492 and 0.498 whatever the flow, tracking the true side's VPIN weakly or not at all (0.06 (se 0.08) in ZI) where the tick rule's correlates 0.43 to 0.88. No cell of section 4 reaches VisualHFT's website band of 0.60 (the highest true-side VPIN is 0.468 and the highest tick-rule VPIN 0.417, at persistence 0.95).
5. **VPIN and toxicity (section 4).** VPIN against toxicity as the informed model defines it, over 14 cells (the informed model's dials, and one-sided uninformed flow with no informed traders) on 8 seeds of 20,000 units each, 112 runs, none of which took V to its floor. **An informed fill costs the maker against V by construction, and the makers still earn**: at the defaults the loss is 2.19 ticks a lot (se 0.03; 10th, 50th and 90th percentiles 0.6, 1.8 and 4.3), the informed share 0.155 (se 0.001) and VPIN 0.168 (tick rule 0.129, BVC 0.493); realized, the loss on an informed fill is 1.03 (se 0.02) at 30 units and -0.28 (se 0.01) at 100 events, where the maker still earns, and over all fills the makers earn 0.757 (se 0.006) a lot at 30 units against 0.910 (se 0.002) with no informed traders; the least in any cell is 0.492 (se 0.009). **The informed share does not fix the earnings**: raising `kappa` from 0.5 to 8 raises the share from 0.112 to 0.180 and lowers the loss on an informed fill from 3.45 to 1.68 (the book tracks V more closely), while raising `sigma_v` from 0 to 1.0 raises both (share 0.123 to 0.257, loss 2.01 to 6.04) and earnings fall from 0.808 to 0.492. **VPIN rises with one-sided flow whether or not anything is behind it**: 0.171, 0.266 and 0.468 at persistence 0.7, 0.85 and 0.95, where no order is informed, and the same VPIN earns opposite amounts (`kappa` 8, VPIN 0.172, earns 0.762 at 30 units; persistence 0.7, VPIN 0.171, earns 0.924; `sigma_v` 1.0, VPIN 0.255, earns 0.492; persistence 0.85, VPIN 0.266, earns 0.921). **Within a run VPIN predicts the next bucket's loss mostly through the recent one-sidedness it averages**: its partial coefficient on the loss at 30 units is 0.045 (se 0.005) beside the bucket's own imbalance and 0.009 (se 0.006) once the last five buckets' mean imbalance is added (which has 0.143 (se 0.015)); at `sigma_v` 1.0 it stays at 0.036 (se 0.014), 2.6 standard errors from 0. At the default the same holds for the informed share of the next bucket's lots: VPIN's partial falls from 0.071 (se 0.014) to 0.013 (se 0.012) once the five-bucket mean is in. **VPIN cannot lead a jump of V, and after one it rises slowly** (rare jumps of 6 ticks, 444 of 771 studied jumps isolated): trailing VPIN is up +0.0043 a bucket after and +0.0132 eight after, while the mid has moved half the jump after a median 10.2 units (within the jump's bucket for 13% of jumps, by the next 43%, by the third 79%). R-002's question is another (VPIN against a volatility forecast), so the two results are not comparable.
6. **How fast the book refills (section 5).** Recovery of the near-touch depth after a depletion (K = 3, depleted below 0.5 and recovered at 0.9 of the baseline), against what the ZI family's refill implies: 15 lots a unit across the 3 prices, so a deficit D would refill in D / 15 units if nothing were taken or canceled. **An episode measured from the side's current best quote is mostly invented**: in ZI it starts 14 times as many episodes as the anchored one, 0.692 (se 0.006) a unit against 0.048 (se 0.001), 99.6% of them from a quote-improving arrival (the factor is 7.0, 5.3, 1.3 and 11.2 in Hawkes ZI, MF 2008, Parlour and Informed ZI). **Depth takes far longer to come back than the gross refill says, and the net refill accounts for it**: in ZI the median recovery is 32.0 units (se 1.4) against 7.3 (se 0.2) from the gross refill; per unit of episode time 13.6 lots arrive at the anchored prices, 9.7 are taken and 1.7 canceled, a net of 2.2, so D over the median net rate is 38.6 units (se 1.4) and the median depth gained over it 29.9 (se 1.1), which bracket the measured 32.0 (the other models hold too: 24.4 against 7.8 in Hawkes ZI, 18.6 against 4.7 in Informed ZI). **In four of the five models most episodes are abandoned before they recover**, the recovered share being 0.41, 0.23, 0.54, 0.07 and 0.33, and the onset changes the answer (in ZI 32.0 units from "below the line" and 17.1 (se 0.8) from "crossing"). **Large's restoration is fast and nearly certain**: a limit order puts the quote back within 30 units after 0.984, 0.979, 0.940, 0.974 and 0.983 of the takes that moved it, after a median 0.58, 0.50, 1.71, 0.79 and 0.51 units, while the depth behind it takes tens.
7. **Order-to-trade ratios (section 6).** Over a window of 10 units, the model's orders only: OTR is 10.22 (se 0.03) messages a trade in ZI, 12.93, 5.43, 2.48 and 8.65 in the others, and TTO 0.166, 0.161, 0.274, 0.448 and 0.191; the RTS 9 form, one lower. **OTR is more related to the unit just past than to the unit to come**: its correlation with the next unit's absolute change is -0.120 (se 0.004) in ZI, with the last unit's -0.158 (se 0.003), and given the last change the partial with the next is -0.103, -0.239, -0.006, -0.112 and -0.086 (se 0.003 to 0.005); more messages per trade comes with smaller moves, and TTO with larger ones (+0.128 in ZI against the next change). **VisualHFT's order-to-trade ratio is a count of book changes**, not either ratio here (its trade count is never incremented).
8. **A taker on the imbalance (7a).** A taker alone in the dashboard's market, over 12,000 units a run on seeds 101 to 112: when it is flat and the absolute touch-weighted L 10 imbalance |I| is at least a threshold it sends a market order of 5 lots in the imbalance's direction and leaves by the clock after a hold; baselines are 0 after costs and a null that enters in a random direction at the same holds (it pays the spread and its own push on the mid). **Only the corner of the grid with the fewest entries pays after costs, and only in ZI**: at |I| >= 0.7 and a hold of 10 it makes 1.36 (se 0.14) a round trip and +270 (se 30) over the run on 196 entries, positive on 12 of 12 seeds (hold 30: 1.79 (se 0.19) and +258 (se 25) on 147 entries; hold 2: -0.76 (se 0.09) and -216 (se 31) on 280, 0 of 12). Two of the 20 cells over both models pay; VisualHFT's R-002 calls the imbalance unprofitable as a standalone taker, and here it is in 18 of 20. **The signal is there before costs, and the spread takes it**: in ZI the round trip before costs less the null's is 4.13 (se 0.07) to 9.45 (se 0.20), 8.72 (se 0.19) in the main cell, which makes 8.96 (se 0.14) before costs and keeps 1.36. **In Informed ZI it loses in every cell**: the main cell makes -2.75 (se 0.14) a round trip and -1,085 (se 62), positive on 0 of 12 seeds, on 5.86 (se 0.15) before costs less the null's against 8.72 in ZI; an independent check found that with the informed traders off (`kappa` 0) the taker's run is ZI's, entry for entry, so they are why. **The main cell is not the best cell of the grid, and a count of positive seeds is not a test.** The cards (seed 101, the main cell): +165 in ZI (fills and record agree, 202 trades) and -910 in Informed ZI (the record -908, 377 trades).
9. **A quote joiner that pulls on VPIN (7b).** A joiner (5 lots at the best bid and ask) that rests nothing while the board's tick-rule VPIN is at or above 0.16, 0.20 or 0.25, against the same joiner that never pulls, in Informed ZI at `sigma_v` 0.3 and 1.0 and in one-sided flow with no informed traders (persistence 0.85), with a placebo that pulls as much, in episodes as long, at times that ignore VPIN. **High VPIN is not a worse time to quote, so pulling on it only costs the time off the book**: in the default cell at 0.16 VPIN is at or above the line 0.28 (se 0.02) of the time and the never-pulling joiner's fills are as good in both regimes (a gain of 0.348 (se 0.008) and 0.352 (se 0.011) a lot at 30 units, 0.163 and 0.174 of its lots informed). **The VPIN rule does no better than the placebo**: the pulled shares match (0.21 and 0.20), and the rule's P&L less the placebo's is -178 (se 733) and -616 (se 737) at the default, -1,152 (se 1,198), +426 (se 1,633) and +155 (se 551) at `sigma_v` 1.0 and +363 (se 356) and -416 (se 394) in the persistence cell, none further than about one standard error from 0; at the default and 0.16 the rule makes -4,079 (se 821) against never pulling and the placebo -3,901 (se 517). **Pulling costs what the time off the book predicts**: 4,548 (se 286) predicted against 4,079 (se 821) measured at the default and 0.16, 21,953 (se 824) against 23,565 (se 1,189) at `sigma_v` 1.0, where the rule pulls 86% of the time. **The joiner's own quotes raise the tick VPIN** (0.146 against 0.129 in the default cell), so a threshold belongs to the market it was set in. The cards (seed 101): +22,210 never pulling and +23,325 pulling at 0.20, one seed; over the twelve the rule at 0.20 makes -79 (se 400) against never pulling.
10. **What it leaves out** (its Limitations, in short). One simulator, five models at one set of parameters and one initial mid: the seeds hold out the settings and not the simulator. The comparison with R-001 is of a simulator's reversal rate and not of Nasdaq's.
