Four of the five models have no information behind their flow, and the informed model has one latent value and one kind of informed trader, a stand-in for Glosten and Milgrom's and not a model of it; what it makes true by construction is not evidence about real markets, and the informed share is not a sufficient statistic (`kappa` raises it and lowers the loss per informed fill, `sigma_v` raises both). The settings come from small grids on seeds 1 to 8 that the notebook does not print, the best cell of a grid is the best of a list fixed in advance, and counts of positive seeds are not tests. The taker uses market orders only, and 7b's joiner, the only passive strategy, has no signal but its pulling rule; each is alone in its market. Time is in units of simulation time and not seconds, so VisualHFT's windows of 100 ms to 60 s do not map onto it; BVC is the bucket-level form with the standard deviation of the last 100 buckets' changes. It does not test the footprint, the tape, the triggers, volume delta as a study, market resilience's bias, or VisualHFT's Book Toxicity Index and Directional Flow Pressure.
11. **How it was checked, and what it costs to run.** The design had two reviews before anything was built, one of its premises (eleven corrections) and one of its feasibility against the code, measured (nineteen), and every correction was adopted; the informed model had a review of its own, whose six problems were fixed. The numbers in the notebook's prose were compared with its stored outputs by script, and the prose reports where an independent check outside the notebook, with its own code, agreed or found more. Each backtest's record makes what its fills made (+165 and +165, -910 and -908). The notebook took 14.5 minutes in its last full run, on 10 workers of the laptop (2026-11-11; the stored outputs are from it), and its prose between the tables is typed, so after a re-run compare it with the new outputs.

## 4. Environment & Execution Guide

### Python Environment
- **Path**: `/Users/mengren/anaconda3/envs/lob-sim/bin/python` (Python 3.14.3)
- **Install**: `make install` (`pip install -e ".[dev]"`). Dependencies live in `pyproject.toml`: core, plus `[web]`, `[notebooks]` (includes `joblib`), `[parquet]` (`pyarrow`, for Parquet downloads) and `[dev]` (all of them, plus `pytest`, `httpx`, `ruff`, `pytailwindcss`, `playwright`) extras. Python ≥ 3.10.

Run `make` targets from an environment with the project installed, or pass the interpreter, e.g. `make check PYTHON=/Users/mengren/anaconda3/envs/lob-sim/bin/python`.

### Checks
```bash
make lint             # ruff
make css-check        # fails if app/static/css/app.css is out of date with the templates
make test             # pytest (2,925 tests, ~8 min: 477 s under nice, on a shared machine)
make notebooks-check  # executes all sixteen notebooks into a temp dir; fails on the first error
make check            # all four (nearly three hours before notebook 13 had its section 6, and longer now on the laptop: 15 minutes of notebook 11 and 71 of notebook 12 on 16 free cores, 62 to 67 of notebook 13 on 16 cores shared with other work before its section 6, and 80 for it whole, with section 6, on the laptop's 15 workers; 14.5 of notebook 14 on 10 workers of the laptop, and 45 of notebook 15 and 23.5 of notebook 16 on 12; NOTEBOOKS="..." runs fewer)
make test-browser     # drives the dashboard in Chrome with Playwright (132 tests; all three engines with make test-browsers took 1,020 s, about 340 s an engine); not part of make check
make test-browsers    # the same in Chrome, Firefox and WebKit (Safari's engine): 396 runs (the last full run passed 392 and skipped 4, two Chrome-only tests in the other two, in 15 min 31 s; WebKit alone, 119 passed and 2 skipped, three times running)
```

The browser tests (`tests/browser`, marked `browser` and skipped by `make test`) start their own server on a free port and use the installed Google Chrome, or Playwright's Chromium (`python -m playwright install chromium`). Each test gets a fresh browser profile, hence a fresh session, and fails on any console error. They cover what the server-side tests cannot: playback buttons and keyboard shortcuts, the logarithmic speed slider, live quick prices and order results, own-order markers, cross-tab control sync (including a form being edited), Fit after dragging the price axis, the stylized-facts panel and notebook 05's values, the views, the themes and the text size, the columns at four window widths and on a phone, your orders and fills on the chart, one-click trading and drag-to-move (with the simulation running), the heatmap (also when it starts over during its load), guided lessons, your P&L and its line, the tick view, strategies and their leaderboard (a click on a row's button while the board is pushed), the market-making lesson, recording and playing back (with a download and an upload), fitting a strategy, the order book from the keyboard, and the Pairs view (its chart filling without a shimmer, following, zooming, dragged back, and dropping its oldest points; a trader learning from the chart; a click on its board while it is pushed; new settings; four tabs on the view; the Trade view catching up afterwards, also when the page opened on Pairs; a lesson's action from there; each kind of trader; a trader fitted over training runs, started, and canceled; the chart's zoom, vertical scale, panes, height and readout), and the Stat-arb view (many books and each kind of trader in them; the four kinds that score every book, with a line a book on the chart and no levels; its market changed, and what is wrong said, a field at fault marked, a drop-down too; its chart's controls; no setting's label cut off in any theme or text size; a tab moving between the views on one stream); and the Trade view's studies (the band's tiles, settings and helps, through a reset, a playback and a seek), tape (its filters, pause and tick-rule marks), footprint (its cells adding up to the bars, widening, the interval and the theme), triggers (a rule that pauses the run, its editor and its marks on the chart) and informed model (its line, Reveal, *not recorded* in a playback), with the Trade view responsive at 2,000 events a second with everything on. Tests that use the mouse on the order book first scroll its rows to the middle of the window: the sticky header covers the top; they click radio buttons' visible labels (a forced click on the hidden input misses in Firefox). `LOB_BROWSERS` (chrome, firefox, webkit) picks the engines, each from `python -m playwright install`; WebKit found two races Chrome's timing hid (see 3A.3 and 3D.3b), so run `make test-browsers` after changing the page's scripts. When driving htmx forms, wait for the swap to settle (`settle()` in the tests): htmx binds new content only after a short delay. Run them with the machine awake: a display that sleeps, an idle sleep or a closed lid in the middle of a run shows as waits that time out after 30 seconds, mostly in WebKit (`caffeinate -dis make test-browsers` holds the first two off on a Mac).

### Front-end Assets
The page loads nothing from the internet: htmx, Lightweight Charts and the Inter and JetBrains Mono fonts are in `app/static` (licenses in `app/static/LICENSES.md`). `app/static/css/themes.css` (the color themes) is generated by `scripts/build_themes.py`, and `app/static/css/app.css` is built by Tailwind CSS v3.4.17 from the classes used in the templates and in the page's own scripts (`app/static/js/lob-*.js`). **After adding or changing classes, run `make css`** (the `pytailwindcss` dev dependency fetches the Tailwind binary on first use); `make check` fails while `app.css` is stale. Static URLs carry the file's modification time (`asset()` in `app/main.py`), so browsers pick up a rebuilt stylesheet without a hard refresh.

### Re-running the Notebooks
`make notebooks` re-executes all sixteen in place, refreshing their stored outputs (`NOTEBOOKS=...` selects a subset). Notebooks 03–09 take about 14, 17, 58, 18, 21, 28 and 28 seconds on this machine, notebook 11 about 15 minutes and notebook 12 about 71 minutes. Notebook 13 took 62 and 67 minutes in two runs on 16 cores shared with other work, before its section 6 was added; with section 6 its last full run took 80 minutes on the laptop with 15 workers (2026-11-09; the stored outputs are from it). An earlier run took 54 minutes on the workstation, a 64-core Threadripper, with 64 workers; its sections 1 to 5, and this run's, printed exactly what the laptop's run before section 6 had. Notebook 14 took 14.5 minutes in its last full run, on 10 workers of the laptop (2026-11-11; the stored outputs are from it). Notebook 15 took 41, 44 and 45 minutes in its three full runs (2026-11-12, 13 and 14), on 12 workers of the laptop and with a per-cell timeout of three hours (`NB_TIMEOUT=10800`; the Makefile's default is 3600); the stored outputs are from the third, whose last lines print 458 core-minutes (397, and 62 in the runs added after the first run). Notebook 16 took 25 and 23.5 minutes in its two full runs (2026-11-14, 17:36 to 18:01 and 20:19 to 20:43), on 12 workers of the laptop with the same per-cell timeout; the stored outputs are from the second, whose last lines print 241 core-minutes (the first run's said 260: the same runs take another time from one run to the next). 05 (32 seeds per model) and 06 (8) run their seeds in parallel, as do 11 to 16. Their conclusions are computed from the runs, so re-running regenerates them; the prose between notebook 13's tables is typed, with its numbers, and so are the seconds it quotes for choosing baskets (timings, which change with the machine and its load), so after a re-run compare them with the new outputs. Notebook 14's prose is typed in the same way (its numbers, and the events a unit of simulation time holds, which are measured on seeds 1 to 8 and set in its setup cell), and so is notebook 15's (its numbers, compared with the headline figures its last cell prints; its timing lines change with the machine and its load) and notebook 16's (written after each run from the figures its cells printed, except the four outcome texts of its test, written before the first run and kept byte-identical; a printed sentence changes only when its cell is run again, and the prose is edited only through its cell files and `prose.py sync`, 3S.12).

### Launching the Server
```bash
make serve            # 127.0.0.1:8000; HOST=... PORT=... to change
```
This runs `uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000 --timeout-graceful-shutdown 5 --timeout-keep-alive 75`. The first timeout matters: open dashboard tabs hold event streams, and without it a server waits for them indefinitely when asked to stop. The second matters to Safari: its engine closes an idle connection after about 30 seconds, and a request it sends just as the server closes the connection first is lost, not sent again as Chrome and Firefox send it. At uvicorn's default of 5 seconds, WebKit lost 14 and 18 of 84 requests sent 4.98 to 5.00 seconds after the last (a click that did nothing, about five seconds after the one before); at 75 it lost none of 168, since it always closes first. Start a server by hand with the same flag. To stop a server, kill only the listener (a plain `lsof -t -i:8000` also matches browsers connected to the port):
```bash
lsof -t -iTCP:8000 -sTCP:LISTEN | xargs kill
```

Sessions live in the server process's memory: each uvicorn process has its own sessions, and a restart starts every visitor afresh (open pages reload themselves). The API has no authentication; keep the default localhost binding unless the network is trusted.

| **Total** | | **2925 + 132 browser** |

**How `lob/statarb.py`, `app/statarb.py` and `app/live.py` were tested for gaps, before the fit.** Each of their 838 operators and constants was changed, one at a time, in a copy of the repository, and the tests run against it. Passing tests had hidden a good deal: a first sweep missed 278 changes, among them the order baskets and p-value pairs are ranked in. The tests now notice 697. The other 141 are annotations, default constants, changes that cannot matter (`old < 1` on a state of -1, 0 or 1), and details of the clock's pace that only a slow timing test could see. That was before the fit panel. The fit's code had a sweep of its own: all of `app/training.py` and `app/statarb.py`, the fit's routes in `app/main.py`, and `warm_up` and `CopulaPortfolio` in `lob/statarb.py`, 358 changes against `tests/test_statarb_fit.py` and the files nearest each module. The tests noticed 277. Of the other 81, five were real gaps, now tested (a deferred copula learning the mids that came meanwhile, a candidate started before any fit, a grid too large to count); the rest are annotations, default constants, changes with no effect, and routes' status codes that other test files hold. Those sweeps were before `lob/crosssection.py` and the flow counters in `lob/statarb.py`, which have had none; `tests/test_crosssection.py` holds them to computations written from the specification instead (a projection by QR, the PCA and the ridge written out again, histories made by hand). `lob/sparse_baskets.py` had a sweep of its own, one change at a time over its comparisons, arithmetic operators, numbers, negations, transposes, `if` tests and `min`, `max` and `sorted` calls: the tests caught 178 of 184, and the other 6 are equivalent (3S.8).

**How the studies, the triggers and the informed model were tested for gaps.** Their tests hold each rule to a computation made in the test and not to the code (a list of every lot for VPIN, numpy's normal CDF for BVC, a look at every event for the windows, the rate rule worked by hand, `np.isin` for the informed ids), and, where nothing may change, to the earlier code: a run with `kappa` 0, `cap` 0 or `persistence` 0.5 against `SmithFarmerModel` event for event, and the OFI increment against `lob.metrics`. Deliberate breaks were run while they were built, on earlier versions of the files: a mechanical sweep of `lob/studies.py` (527 changes, 49 not noticed, which led to more tests; 540 changes on a later version, 5 not noticed), hand-picked breaks of the session's code, the triggers and the page's scripts, and 37 of the informed model, all noticed. **None was repeated on the final files**, and `lob/studies.py`, `app/triggers.py` and `lob/models/informed.py` changed after their sweeps; hand-picked sweeps flattered the tests here before (the first sweep of `lob/statarb.py` missed 278 of 838), so these are weaker evidence than the mechanical ones above.

**How stage 4 was tested for gaps.** Each part of the library was broken by hand on a copy of the repository as it was built, 130 breaks in all (20 for the shift, `book_cash`, `check`, `exposures()`, the scoring and the Johansen guard, 26 for the trend and the index book, 23 for deals, 23 for index arbitrage and 38 for the deal traders), and the proof against 42ae383 was itself broken seven ways (3S.9). Every break was noticed, but for two in the deal traders, which two new tests and a widened one now catch. Hand-picked breaks flattered the tests here before, so a mechanical sweep followed, on copies, one change at a time: every arithmetic operator, comparison (turned round and moved to its boundary), `and` and `or`, `True`, `False` and `None`, number, `not`, `is` and `in` in `lob/deals.py`, `lob/deal_traders.py` and `lob/indexarb.py` and in the lines that `lob/statarb.py` gained: 613 changes (53, 96, 100 and 364; the 4 on f-string lines of raises, 17 annotations and 62 that were not valid Python were left out and counted). 44 survived at first. **27 were real gaps, now closed** by 23 new test functions and one existing test extended; 17 changed no behavior, and one of those was pinned all the same, leaving 16 that no input can reach (a default that its only caller passes, a private value that is set before it is read, a change that leaves every returned value as it was). So 597 of the 613 are noticed now, and the other 16 have no effect. Examples of real gaps: `rcond=None` changed to 0 in `index_basket`'s least squares, which only a near tie between a book and a copy of one shows (the test was found by a search over designs); `/` changed to `*` in the floor's push in a market with an index, which only loadings other than 1 show; a break time equal to a grid point or to the deal's end, and a uniform exactly equal to `rate * step`, each of which needed a stand-in generator to supply the one draw; and the index's gap of exactly half a tick. The sweep found nothing wrong in the code, so each real gap was a missing test. It ran before the fix pass (3S.9: a broken deal's books kept out until its planned end, the quarantine's default, and `acquirers`), whose changes have tests of their own, each shown to fail with the old rule put back on a copy (30 tests fail for the cooling rule), and have not been swept.

The Stat-arb view had the same. Its builders made 35 hand-picked breaks (9, 11, 7 and 8, the last on the fixes), every one noticed; then a sweep of the lines that `app/statarb.py` and `app/main.py` gained or changed (every token, the opposite of every condition, and the raise and f-string lines; 22 changes that were not valid Python left out): 249 changes, 26 survived at first, **21 were real gaps, now closed** by seven tests added to `tests/test_statarb_view.py`, and the other 5 have no effect (the `step` of an integer setting, printed as 1 whatever it is; a `strict=True` that cannot fire; a popped default). Examples: the page's ranges and steps (13 changes), which the page tests had compared with the module's own `Param`s, so that a range that moved moved both; the chooser thread given to the index trader by `A and not B` turned into `A or not B`, so that it chose one decision late; the tally of ended deals skipping the first whenever the first look came after one had; and a deal's gap printed to the unit and not to the tenth. The one defect the audit found in behavior came from driving the old and the new code with the same requests and not from the sweep: with every new setting off a market of 16 books differed from 42ae383 (`MAX_SIGNALS` had gone from 12 to 13). This sweep too ran before the view's fix pass (`signal_cap`, the fit route's question of what the market lacks, the templates' whitespace), which was held by 8 hand-picked breaks and by new tests and not swept, and the page's scripts and templates were checked by the browser tests (7 breaks of their own) and by looking at the page in three engines, not by a sweep.

---

## 6. Changes on the `review-fixes` Branch (2026-11-02)

**Review corrections.**
- **Models**: the ZI model follows the paper's placement rule; the fractional-noise generator no longer halves every autocorrelation; Mike-Farmer rounds bids and asks symmetrically (it had biased 55% of mid-price moves downward).
- **Notebooks**: all six re-executed; conclusions are computed with stated criteria rather than typed in; the concave-impact, bid-ask-bounce and long-memory-sign ablation cells were redone; quotations checked against the paper.
- **Dashboard**: fabricated candle history removed; bars built from trades on one simulation clock; engine toggle and multi-tab chart reset fixed; input validation added.

**Improvements.**
1. **Order book**: orders are validated before matching (an off-tick crossing order used to execute its fills and then crash), and duplicate order IDs are rejected (they corrupted the order index).
2. **Dashboard robustness**: one event stream per tab instead of two; a real connection indicator; pages reload when their simulation is gone; no more synchronous request logging to `/tmp/lob_app.log`; `make serve` binds to localhost with a graceful-shutdown timeout.
3. **Packaging**: `pyproject.toml` with extras, `ruff`, and `make check`.
4. **Notebooks**: seeds run in parallel (joblib). Notebook 05 uses 32 seeds per model and shows a 95% confidence interval in every scorecard cell, with a computed note on any verdict left open; notebook 06 uses 8.
5. **Simulation**: `Simulation.trades` records every fill; the event log uses typed arrays.
6. **Mike-Farmer signs**: a continuous long-memory stream instead of independent 50,000-sign blocks.
7. **Dashboard parameters**: users set each model's parameters and the seed; Reset replays a run exactly.
8. **Mike-Farmer (2008 structure) model**: reproduces 6 of the 7 stylized facts in notebook 05, including heavy tails, the hump-shaped depth profile and concave impact; its volatility clustering sits on the bar.
9. **Hawkes ZI model**: self-exciting market orders produce volatility clustering (rank ACF of |r| 0.24 vs 0.05 for ZI in notebook 05) and heavy tails; notebook 06 shows it is the only tested ingredient that raises clustering.
10. **One simulation per browser session.**

**Follow-ups.**
- Pro-rata matching allocates in whole lots (with lots above 1 it used to leave resting orders that were not whole lots).
- Model, parameters, seed, engine and speed stay in sync across tabs of a session.
- Notebook 05's verdicts: 32 seeds, confidence intervals, and computed explanations for open verdicts.

**Dashboard revisions.**
- Inaccurate text fixed: the notes card (three of six models, an unverified ">150k events/sec with O(1) depth tracking" claim) is now a reading guide; the header links to the paper instead of a generic GitHub topic; trades are labeled BUY/SELL by aggressor.
- Order entry: quick prices follow the book (they showed page-load prices); every order reports its result.
- Layout: compact controls and metrics bring the price chart above the fold; rows wrap on phones instead of overflowing; the chart header no longer repeats the zoom toolbar's buttons.
- Logarithmic speed slider with the active preset highlighted; keyboard shortcuts; own resting orders marked in the DOM.

**Remaining roadmap items (2026-11-03).** Fill notices; split orders (TWAP) compared with one sweep and the market's VWAP; notebook 07 (OFI across models and depths: OFI explains 10% to 61% of 100-event price changes; in the ZI model the slope falls as depth^-2.3); `lob.calibration` and notebook 08 (every Mike & Farmer parameter recovered from simulated runs).

**Dashboard interface (2026-11-03 and 04).** Color themes (Midnight, Daylight, Terminal, color-blind-safe buy/sell); Trade, Analyze and Learn views; the user's resting orders and fills on the price chart; a depth chart under the order book; one-click trading and drag-to-move in the book, and Cancel all; a smaller default text size with a Text setting, and the full width in up to four columns; an order-book heatmap; five guided lessons (Section 3).

**What notebook 15 left open, notebook 16 (2026-11-14).** Notebook 15's three unsettled things in a notebook of their own, with no change to the library (3S.12): reading the news on a trader's own pairs, why the pairs chosen in the market with a risk premium are worth less, and sector index books. The order of work: a map of the code and a design, a premise review of the design before anything was built, a plan and builders' notes, the notebook as one file a cell (`build.py`), smoke runs on seeds 1 to 6 and an audit of its code on seeds that are not its own, the first full run on seeds 101 and up with the test's four outcome texts written before it; a premise skeptic, a numbers audit, a judgment reader and a reproduction of seven figures read it; the second full run corrected ten printed lines and added two parts that print what the skeptic measured (the lots held and the cost of reading; the 24 new seeds against the 200); and a second numbers check, a second judgment reader and one fix pass of the markdown. The test is met on 24 new seeds (+576, se 116, t 4.98, 19 of 24 seeds positive); the route shows notebook 15's gap on 200 seeds before costs, and the 24 new seeds do not show it in the market (+38, se 177) or on the route (-94, se 186), with no cause found; and sector indexes do not pay at this market's price of an index lot (2.98 book lots against a break-even of 2). What stays open is a chooser that reads the news and re-bases a target after a completion. Tests: none added, and nothing in `lob/`, `app/` or `tests/` was changed for it.

**More long/short strategies, stage 4 (2026-11-12).** The last three of the eight strategies agreed with the user, for which the market of notebooks 12 and 13 had nothing to trade: index arbitrage, a merger-style trade on stock deals, and momentum that can pay. `FactorValues` has three options, each off by default and each with a random stream of its own whose draws depend on time alone: a trend of the factors (an Ornstein-Uhlenbeck drift), an index book (one more book, the weighted sum of the others and a premium of its own) and stock deals (an acquirer offers shares for a target's, and the deal may break). `BookMarket` moves a book's resting orders when its fair value jumps, so that a jump is not free money; `lob/indexarb.py` and `lob/deal_traders.py` have the traders; and `score_holdings` reports a trader's risk over 100 units and the part of its P&L that the factors made (3S.9). With all three off the market is that of 42ae383 to the tick (104 comparisons in a proof against the old code, and a test of it in the suite). The order of work: a design; a premise review of it before anything was built (12 verdicts, of which one held as written; among the others, that the index's scale decides whether replication pays, that no rule that trades a spread of mids earns nothing before costs, so that no control can be judged by it, that a book must shift with a jump or the first trader takes it for nothing, and that a deal's acquirer should be drawn within the target's sector) and a feasibility map of the code (what each change touches, where a draw could move, and that Johansen's test has critical values for 12 books at most), which together turned the design into a sheet; the library by parts, each tested with deliberate breaks (130, and the proof broken seven ways); an audit of the built code, which found no code that did other than the sheet said but one premise of the sheet that did not hold as built (the dial of `breaks` was not the same deals at every value, since a broken deal had freed its books early: they now stay out until its planned end plus the cooling time), and a mechanical sweep of the library (613 changes, 44 survivors at first, 27 of them real gaps, now closed); an exploration of the three dials on seeds 1 to 8, by three explorers, and a triage that reran eight of its cells, found no bug in the library and corrected 15 of the explorers' readings (among them that a sample of the index's books needs a formation of 2,000 decisions and not 400); the Stat-arb view (nine settings and two kinds, 3S.11), built in four parts, audited, swept (249 changes, 26 survivors at first, 21 real gaps, now closed) and repaired; and notebook 15, whose findings are in 3S.10. Notebook 15 was written from settings and a run plan taken from an exploration on seeds 1 to 8 and a triage of it; its code was audited before it was first run on the reporting seeds, and its prose was written from the stored outputs. A numbers audit and a premise skeptic, which reproduced figures from its own accounts, then read it against those outputs. Two reruns added prints and three cells (the later one the prints that bring the skeptic's checks, made outside the notebook, inside it), and each printed again, to the digit, every line of the run before it but for timing lines and 16 lines relabeled on purpose; the notebook holds the third full run's outputs (2026-11-14). Found on the way: Johansen's test cannot be read in more than 12 books, so that the market of 4 sectors of 4 books was already wrong at 42ae383 (it is now refused); `score_portfolio`'s risk, over one unit of time, flatters a trader that holds a factor and shortchanges a neutral one, so `score_holdings` reports it over 100; a `DealTrader`'s records at the end of a run lean toward broken deals; the first build of the view had changed a market of 16 books with every new setting off (`MAX_SIGNALS` from 12 to 13), found by driving the old and the new code with the same requests, and repaired; and a pair that holds the index as one of its books can hold its book alone at a small size, since the index's leg rounds to no lot (3S.11). Tests: six new files, additions to three more and seven new browser tests (section 5).

**The Baskets default (2026-11-11).** At the user's word, after stage 3, the Stat-arb view's Baskets kind defaults to an entry of 1 and a trade rate of 0.25 (it was an entry of 2, going all the way), notebook 13's section 6's best for Johansen's baskets of 3: +2,044 (se 132) and +2,012 (se 176) more than the old default in the sectors and mixed markets, on more than two and a half times the lots (3S.8). Johansen is still the chooser; pairs, residuals, copula pairs and the other kinds are unchanged, and pairs keep an entry of 2 and a rate of 1. The summary and the board's description say so, and the 11 test cases that pinned the old default were updated, with one test and a page check added (each checked to fail with the old default put back). Notebook 13's markdown says that the entry-2 row was the dashboard's default when section 6 was run; its numbers and printed outputs are as they were.

**Microstructure studies, triggers and notebook 14 (2026-11-11).** Inspired by VisualHFT (visualhft.com; github.com/visualHFT/VisualHFT, Apache License 2.0), whose code was read at commit b36b9a2 and whose research notes R-001 and R-002 the notebook tests. `lob/studies.py` has eight studies of the stream of book events (OFI, CVD, delta of trades, VPIN, order-to-trade ratios, book imbalance, spread and depth resilience), run by a board in each session and on a `Simulation` through its new `observer`; the Trade view has a band of tiles for them, each with its help and settings, a footprint on the candle chart, a tape with a fill number that is never reused, filters, a pause and tick-rule marks, and a card of trigger rules that notice, pause, cancel your orders or flatten you (3T). Definitions follow VisualHFT's code where the docstrings say so, and where its code differs from its website the studies follow the papers and the page says so: its OTR counts book changes (its trade count is never incremented), its VPIN classifies against the mid, and its bands are on its website only (3T.9). The board costs about 3 microseconds an event in ZI (+36%; about 7 in Parlour), is off in fits and collections, and in a seek except for its last 40,000 events. A sixth model, ZI with informed traders (`lob/models/informed.py`), gives toxicity a ground truth: a hidden value that informed traders trade on, their share of the taker lots (0.155 at the defaults) and what makers lose to them, with a Reveal switch that draws the value on the chart and a persistence setting for flow that is one-sided without being toxic. Notebook 14 asks of six studies what VisualHFT's research asks of its own, scored against the truth and against the baselines it names (3U). The queue imbalance beats the reversal baseline by 5.6 to 11.1 pp in every model, and 50% flatters it; OFI explains the interval it falls in and forecasts almost nothing beyond the last change in ZI; the quote rule is exact at lag 0 by construction, and BVC tracks the true buy share far worse than the tick rule; VPIN rises with one-sided flow whether or not anything is behind it (the same VPIN, 0.17, earns the makers 0.762 or 0.924 a lot); the depth takes 32.0 units to come back in ZI against 7.3 from the gross refill, which the net refill accounts for; a taker on the imbalance pays after costs only in the corner of its grid with the fewest entries (+270 (se 30), 12 of 12 seeds, in ZI and -1,085 (se 62), 0 of 12, in Informed ZI); and a quote joiner that pulls its quotes on VPIN does no better than a placebo that pulls as much. Found on the way: the exploration's initial mid of 100 let the informed model's value reach its floor in some runs of 12,000 units (the notebook starts at 10,000); the look-ahead trap of R-002 flips sign here (24.9% agreement against 70.9% causal); and the reviews' "about 12% dropped in ZI" belonged to the inclusive design (10.2%) and not to the strict one that is scored (17.3%). The design had two reviews before anything was built, of 11 and 19 corrections, all adopted. Also: `ofi_increment` moved from `app/facts.py` to `lob/studies.py`, `zero_intelligence.py` has a `_sample_market_side` hook (its output unchanged), and a recording's start carries VPIN's bucket. 2,271 tests (469 new) and 121 browser tests (41 new); notebook 14 took 14.5 minutes on 10 workers of the laptop. Found on the way, in the chart as it already was: a click in Safari about five seconds after the last request could be lost, because the server closed idle connections after uvicorn's default of 5 seconds and Safari's engine does not send such a request again (the server now keeps them 75 seconds: section 4, Launching the Server); and a bar pushed before a reset or a change of interval could be drawn after the reload that followed it, since the two come on different connections (each bar now says which version of the chart it belongs to, 3C.8). Both showed as one browser test in about 120 failing in WebKit, a different one each run; a test that waited for `currentInterval`, which changes at the click, now waits for `loadedInterval`. A third: two speeds asked for within milliseconds (a preset, then the slider) could reach the server in either order, so the page now sends a speed only when the one before it has been answered (`updateSimSpeed` in `base.html`).

**More long/short strategies, stage 3 (2026-11-09).** Sparse baskets: `lob/sparse_baskets.py` chooses baskets by Box and Tiao's predictability (the portfolios of a few books that a VAR(1) on the levels predicts least, grown greedily from every pair after d'Aspremont 2011) and not by Johansen's test, and notebook 13's section 6 asks what each chooser picks and how the baskets trade (3S.8). Box-Tiao's baskets are the least predictable at 3 legs and more, but by little (0.885 against 0.891 for baskets of 3 books in the sectors market; its pairs are not less predictable than pairs by distance there, 0.901 against 0.899), choose more than ten times as fast (0.01 seconds against 0.23 in the sectors market, timed on the laptop), and at the same settings (an entry of 1, a trade rate of 0.25) earn about what Johansen's do: Box-Tiao less Johansen, paired by seed, is +156 (se 180) and +367 (se 226) for baskets of 3 books in the sectors and mixed markets. The settings matter more: Johansen's baskets of 3 at an entry of 1 and a rate of 0.25 earn +2,044 (se 132) and +2,012 (se 176) more than the dashboard's default then (an entry of 2, the whole way), and became the default on 2026-11-11 (see that day's entry). The Baskets kind on the Stat-arb view has a *Chosen by* choice, Johansen's test by default. Found on the way: Johansen's chooser can hand a trader a basket with no relation in it (it takes first the baskets that share no book with a better one, so with four baskets of 3 from twelve books its fourth can be a triple apart from the first three, taken whenever its trace statistic passes the 95% value); in the picks of eight seeds none of its baskets of 3 leaves more than 0.050 in the sectors market, and the largest in the mixed is 0.548. The library had a premise review of its specification before it was built, has 88 tests, and a mechanical sweep of its lines caught 178 of 184 changes, the other 6 equivalent. Also: the Stat-arb view's reversal default changed to section 5's best, at the user's word (a look back of 50, a hold of 1 and a trade rate of 0.1, where it was a look back of 20 held for 100), and is documented in 3S.5 and 3S.7. This is stage 3 of four.

**More long/short strategies, stage 2 (2026-11-08).** Trading the same signals less: every trader that holds lots in many books can go only a `rate` of the way to its target at a decision and stay put within a `band` of lots of its aim (`BookTrader._toward`, after Gârleanu and Pedersen 2013 and Davis and Norman 1990; 3S.7), and notebook 13's section 5 asks what that does to the traders of sections 3 and 4 and of notebook 12. Of the four cross-sectional signals only reversal pays, as before, but at a look back of 50 it makes +3,485 held for 25 decisions and +4,364 at a rate of 0.1, against +968 for section 3's; the queues, lead-lag and the trades still lose, however they are traded less. For notebook 12's traders in the sectors market a rate or a band adds most to the residuals (+7,223 with a rate and +6,768 with a band, against +4,625 entering at 1.25 alone and +3,952 for section 3's row) and little to pairs (+832 for a rate and -418 for a band, against +7,808 entering at 1.25 alone). Every kind on the Stat-arb view has a trade rate and a no-trade band, 1 and 0 by default, and a fit can try them; the defaults leave every trader as it was (a proof over 144 trader comparisons in 78 markets found none differing). Also: what netting saved is counted against what the states last asked for, as against the position it would have counted lots not yet traded; `entries` counts the states asked for and not the trades; and the residuals' card with a band holds 1.10 times its capital at its most, the first card here above 1. This is stage 2 of four.

**More long/short strategies, stage 1 (2026-11-08).** `lob/crosssection.py` and notebook 13: reversal, momentum, lead-lag and order flow, each holding a book of longs and shorts neutral to the dollar and to estimated factors, in notebook 12's markets (3S). In these books each signal knows something at its own horizon, but none pays for the turnover of a rebalance at every decision, and only a slow reversal pays after costs: +968 and +868, against +5,706 and +3,283 for eight pairs. They are four more kinds on the Stat-arb view (3S.5). `BookMarket` keeps the models' signed flow and volume and a trader can `observe` the market, which leaves notebook 12's markets unchanged to the tick. Found on the way: removing the second of two traders started alike from the Stat-arb board raised an error, since equal dataclasses compare their positions (it now removes by identity); a field at fault was flagged with `aria-invalid` but not marked, as Tailwind 3 has no such variant until `tailwind.config.js` adds it (a test now checks that every aria variant the page uses is in the stylesheet); the board counted *holding n of 12* against the twelve relations it lists and not all of them; and a setting's label in a narrow column wraps to two lines instead of being cut off in the Terminal theme's wider font. This is stage 1 of four.

**Fitting on the Stat-arb view (2026-11-07).** The Stat-arb view fits any kind of trader over training runs of its own, in the Pairs view's panel, with the fitting both views share now in `app/training.py`; notebook 12's section 10 tests that fit on seeds none of it saw (3R.7). Where a trader's starting settings were off the best of the grid the fit gained, by P&L, +2,168 for pairs and +1,411 for baskets in the sectors market; where they were first there was little to find; and *clearly better* was right each time it spoke, in 13 of 24 fits.

**Many books and stat-arb (2026-11-06).** `lob/statarb.py` and notebook 12: many order books with several common factors on a random stream of their own; Johansen baskets, stat-arb on PCA residuals after Avellaneda and Lee (2010), and many pairs by z-score or copula, on real books and seeds none of the settings was chosen on, after costs and per unit of risk (3R). Then the Stat-arb view (3R.6), with what the two views' markets and charts share moved to `app/live.py` and `lob-panes.js`. A last look at it found the charts of both views freezing when a first trader started on a long history (3Q.5).

**A floor for the fair values (2026-11-06).** Left running long enough, the Pairs view's fair values went negative and its books collapsed; they now stop at 10 ticks, which leaves the gap alone (3Q.12). Notebook 10 had met the same flaw, and some of its numbers changed.

**Fitting a pairs trader (2026-11-05).** Any kind of trader on the Pairs view fitted over training runs of its own, each candidate against the user's settings with a standard error, saying plainly when the best is not clearly better (3Q.10), and notebook 11's section 6 on whether that helps on markets the fit has not seen (3Q.11). What a fit tries moved to `app/fitting.py`, shared with the Trade view. Also a race found by the full browser run, from the recording work: bars pushed during a chart reload (3C.8).

**A dynamic hedge and a copula (2026-11-05).** A Kalman trader and a copula trader in `lob/pairs.py` and `lob/copulas.py`, with a drifting beta and a stressed regime to give them something to find, on the Pairs view and in notebook 11; better controls for the view's chart (3Q.5 to 3Q.9).

**The Pairs view (2026-11-05).** The two books of notebook 10 on the dashboard (3Q.5): a market of its own with its settings, an arbitrageur if wanted, and up to six pairs traders, with the gap, their bands and entries, and their P&L as it happens.

**Two books for pairs trading (2026-11-05).** `lob/pairs.py` and notebook 10: two order books whose gap mean-reverts by construction, or wanders while an arbitrageur trades it back; what Engle-Granger, a least-squares hedge ratio and an AR(1) half-life make of them; a pairs trade in the books, with quant-plots' scorecard and signal chart. Two fixes found on the way: mean reversion with an exit of 0 never went flat (now flat at the average), and strategies canceled their orders in an order that depended on every order id used before in the process, so a seed did not always replay a run (now in the order placed).

**Is the flow paying? (2026-11-05).** The leaderboard says whether the order flow pays resting orders (the spread against the mid's move per trade, after Wyart et al. 2008), a quote joiner is the null for making markets, and every row holds its fills against a null with no skill (3P).

**More strategies and fitting (2026-11-04).** Five more strategies (Avellaneda-Stoikov, penny jumping, breakout, order flow imbalance and a random benchmark), up to twelve at once, and fitting any strategy's settings over a training period, a fresh run or a recording, with its training record kept beside its live P&L (3M, 3O).

**Recording and playback (2026-11-04).** Record a session and play it back exactly, at any speed and with a timeline; trade or run strategies on a playback; carry on live from any point; keep recordings as `.npz` files (3N).

**Strategies (2026-11-04).** The market-making bot became one of four strategies the user can run, up to eight at once (3M): market making, momentum, mean reversion and queue imbalance, trading by crossing the spread or resting at the best quote, ranked with the user on a live leaderboard; a second lesson on trading a signal.

**Six more (2026-11-04).** A review of the interface work and its fixes (a heatmap that froze after a reset during its load, gaps in the heatmap's columns, the cancel endpoint canceling the models' orders, and smaller ones); your position, P&L and place in the queue (3L); a tick view of the order book (3D); a market-making bot and its lesson (3M); notebook 09 (metaorder impact: about linear and permanent in every model, not the square root); `lob.lobster` and notebook 08's section 3 (the estimates from data in LOBSTER's format); the order book from the keyboard; and the browser tests in Firefox and WebKit, which found two races.

**Roadmap items (2026-11-03).** Order flow imbalance (library, simulation columns, live card); iceberg orders (both engines, dashboard form); the session VWAP (chart line, order benchmark); CSV and Parquet export of trades and bars. Also: the models never cancel the user's orders, and manual market orders carry price protection.

## 7. Known Limitations

- Sessions are held in memory: lost on restart, separate per server process, capped at 50 (least recently seen evicted) and evicted after 30 minutes idle without an open tab.
- The simplified Mike-Farmer model concentrates depth at the best quote and its book is too thin to measure impact; use the 2008-structure model for those questions.
- Two verdicts in notebook 05 stay open because their values sit next to the bar. Mike-Farmer (2008 structure) volatility clustering is 0.099 against a bar of 0.1, so more seeds will not settle it. Parlour's excess kurtosis is 0.95 against a bar of 1; settling that would take about 915 seeds, 29 times the 32 used. The notebook explains both.
- The stylized-facts panel measures one run: its standard errors are approximate (too small under long memory), and with the dashboard's default rates the Hawkes model's clustering sits on the bar. Use notebook 05's values and 2M events to compare with the notebook.
- The Mike & Farmer (2008) estimators are checked on simulated data only, also in LOBSTER's format: the repository has no order-level market data (LOBSTER's free samples come through a request form). Notebook 08 fits any LOBSTER files put in `data/lobster/` (ignored by git); real data also have varying sizes, hidden orders, partial cancellations and intraday seasonality, which the model lacks.
- The order book's levels view lists only levels with orders; the tick view covers 40 ticks either side of the mid. One-click orders are plain limit orders (no iceberg peak).
- No model reproduces the square-root law of metaorder impact: impact grows about linearly (faster in Mike & Farmer's) and does not revert (notebook 09). Their order flow does not react to a metaorder.
- Recordings live in the session's memory (three at once, 500,000 events each) until downloaded. In a playback the recorded traders do not react to the user's or the strategies' orders; split orders replay as their slices; replayed strategies' signals are recomputed by the current code while their orders come from the tape; Exit playback starts the model afresh on the book.
- The models' order flow pays liquidity providers without skill (3P): a resting strategy's P&L is mostly the flow's, as Vs null and the regime line show. The models' orders carry no information about future prices, as real ones can; replaying real LOBSTER days, a queue-reactive model (Huang, Lehalle and Rosenbaum 2015) or latency would make a fairer test.
- Notebook 12's books all have the same volatility and half-life, so all relations revert at the same speed and Johansen's vectors are arbitrary within their space. Its traders are alone, with market orders only, and twelve books are few for stat-arb: what breadth and netting do at a thousand names once a day is not tested. Its defaults are what did best on notebook 12's seeds 1 to 8, in its two markets (but baskets', now notebook 13's section 6's best, 3S.8), and the grids the Stat-arb fit searches were set on the same seeds, so where a default is first of its grid the fit has little to find (baskets' entry is now first of its grid); it tries them on seeds the live market never has, and three fits to a row cannot say how often *clearly better* is right (3R.7).
- Notebook 13 (3S) has one type of market, twelve books with two tables of loadings, and its traders use market orders only, each alone; passive orders could change every comparison that costs decide, and are where a signal right for a few units of time might pay. Its settings come from grids on seeds 1 to 8: small ones in sections 3 and 4 (up to three a kind, one for momentum), and larger ones, which the notebook does not print, for section 5's test, whose frontier shows the best of three to five points along a curve on the eight seeds that report it. So the best of a kind or a curve is the best of a few, and its counts of settings above two standard errors are over a list fixed in advance, not tests. At high turnover the before-cost figure holds a trader's own impact on the mid it is marked at, so it is not the worth of a signal (section 2's listeners measure that without trading). The order flow is known exactly from the model, which says whether each order bought or sold, where a real trader infers the aggressor from quotes and prints, with errors; the counters hold only the models' orders that traded on arrival, not the traders' own, nor cancellations or resting orders.
- Notebook 13's section 5 (3S.7) has a frontier in one market, the sectors, on eight seeds, and a test of four families and not seven (momentum, which loses in section 3, is not in it). Its aim is not Gârleanu and Pedersen's: it is the trader's own rule's, scaled to its size and not scaled down by the signal's decay or the costs, so the rate and the band show what going part of the way and a band do to these traders, and not what their costs make best. Most of its paired differences compare a family's bests, chosen on seeds 1 to 8, and not one setting changed, and the best of a curve is the best of three to five points on the seeds that report it. The residuals' best rate (entering beyond 0.5, at 0.1) and the queues' (0.02) were at the edge of what was tried on seeds 1 to 8, so a lower one might do better. A band roughly doubles the residuals' exposure to the factors, from 1.6 and 1.9 lots in section 3's rows to 3.8 and 3.5 in the sectors and mixed markets, and the residuals' card with a band holds 1.10 times its capital at its most (the capital of 150,000 is meant to be more than a card holds at its most). A band applies to each book alone, so one leg of a relation can sit inside it while another trades; with a rate or a band `entries` count the states asked for, not the trades. The Pairs view's traders (`lob/pairs.py`) have neither setting.
- Notebook 13's section 6 (3S.8) compares two choosers of baskets of three and four books from twelve (and Box-Tiao's of all twelve, and pairs) in the same two markets. Box-Tiao's chooser tests nothing: it gives the baskets asked for whether or not any is a relation, where Johansen's gives only those its trace test accepts. Predictability is scale-free and does not see a basket's swing against what its legs cost to trade (the least predictable chooser, the baskets of all twelve books, swings least per lot), and the variance threshold of Cuturi and d'Aspremont (2013) is not added; it also rewards unpredictable noise in the mids as much as mean reversion of the fair values, which is dormant here. The search is greedy, from every pair, and not exhaustive, so it can miss the least predictable set of its size, and it fits one lag. Each chooser's entry and rate were chosen on seeds 1 to 8 from a small grid (four entries and three rates, which the notebook does not print), except the baskets row that was the dashboard's default then (an entry of 2, no rate) and Box-Tiao's 4 of 4 at an entry of 1, so a chooser's own best is not like for like with another's. Johansen's chooser can hand the trader a basket with no relation in it (it takes first the baskets that share no book with a better one, so its fourth of four baskets of 3 from twelve books can be a triple apart from the first three, taken whenever its trace statistic passes the 95% value); the picks show none of its baskets of 3 leaving more than 0.050 in the sectors market and 0.548 at most in the mixed, and the notebook does not show how often a trader on other seeds holds one or what it does to its P&L, so that the two choosers earn about the same at the same settings holds for the seeds drawn, and may not for others. What the choosers pick is from one history a seed, the one a trader passes to its chooser at its first choice (the traded baskets, checked by an assert on seed 101 for Box-Tiao's 4 of 3), on eight of the twelve seeds the tables use; the seconds to choose are the laptop's, medians of one call on one core while other seeds' choices ran, to compare the choosers with each other. The Stat-arb fit's grids for the rate and the band of baskets (3S.7) were not set from section 6, and a fit keeps the choice of chooser and tries the numbers: that a fit of Box-Tiao baskets works is tested, and not whether it helps.
- The Stat-arb view's four cross-sectional kinds are tested for how they work, and not for whether fitting them helps: notebook 13 does not fit them, and only reversal pays (its default is now section 5's best, with a rate; order flow and lead-lag still lose at every setting tried). What a fit proposes for the rate and the band of every kind (3S.7) was set from notebook 13's section 5 on seeds 1 to 8, which has no baskets, copula pairs or momentum, and no fit over those values has been tested for any kind. A flow trader has no past flow or queues to learn from and waits to see them once it starts. Lead-lag's default (hold 20, every 1) is the setting of its kind that loses most.
- Fitting a trader on the Pairs or Stat-arb view runs each candidate alone, where on the chart it trades among whatever else is there, and compares them on seeds they share only until the first trade (3Q.11). *Clearly better* does not allow for having picked the best of many, and each panel says so: with nine settings truly equal it says *clearly better* in 27.8% of fits (3R.7).
- Fitting replays its training period without the market reacting to the candidate, as a backtest on historical data does; a fresh training run starts the model's own state over (the Hawkes intensity, the long-memory signs) on the session's book. A fit runs one at a time per session, without a way to stop it once started.
- Strategies decide only as model events arrive, at their interval (so not in the manual model), and in these models nobody trades on information, so stale quotes cost little; P&L is in ticks times lots, without fees. They also trade during a facts collection, which then measures the market with them in it. A strategy sees only the best quotes and the lots at them.
- The heatmap covers 30 ticks either side of the mid and the last 600 units of simulation time, and starts over after a reset or a collection.
- The studies (3T) have been run on the simulator only: no real feed, and no LOBSTER day, has gone through them. Their windows are in units of simulation time and not seconds, and a unit holds 13 to 60 events by model, so VisualHFT's windows of 100 ms to 60 s do not map onto them. Their defaults (a VPIN bucket of 250 lots, a touch of 3 prices, a baseline of 100 units) were set for the ZI family's lots of 5 and its depth; the simplified Mike and Farmer model's queues hold hundreds of lots, so its resilience tile says *no depletions*, and in ZI a side is depleted most of the time, so *depleted now* is the usual state there. A playback starts its studies cold, and a long seek starts them over 40,000 events before its target, so a setting that needs a longer window (VPIN with more than about 80 buckets in ZI) reads *warming up* after one until it has filled again. A playback's VPIN uses the recording's V, and an older recording calibrates its own. Quote-rule accuracy is 100% at lag 0 by construction, so the tile's accuracy means something only at a lag of 1 or more.
- The tape keeps the last 2,000 fills. The footprint keeps the lots at each price of the last 12,000 bars (older bars have none), sends the last 300 buckets of the chart, and the page holds 2,000 bars; it is legible at intervals of about 15 units or more, and the numbers need bars about 40 px apart. Neither is tested in notebook 14, and the footprint's imbalance marks (a ratio of 3 and 10 lots) follow VisualHFT's website: its code has no footprint. Firefox and WebKit last ran the browser tests when there were 110 a browser (328 passed, 2 skipped); the 9 added since have run in Chrome only.
- Triggers (3T.7) are checked after each step of the model and each action of the user's, in simulation time: a cooldown of 5 units is about a sixth of a second at top speed, so a rule with that cooldown could fire about six times a second, and its notices are held to one a second, with the count of firings; a crossing is read between the evaluated values, which for a study that reads the book are at most 0.1 unit apart. They act on the user's account only (cancel all, a protected market order for flatten), do nothing in the manual model (nothing steps), and in a playback only notice and pause: the recording holds the orders of the session it was made from. The notebook does not test them; the quote joiner of its section 7b that pulls on VPIN is its own strategy and not a trigger action. VisualHFT's engine also has a webhook action, and its model has sustained-condition windows (its interface does not): neither is built here.
- The informed model (3T.8) has one latent value, a random walk with jumps held at or above 20 ticks, and one kind of informed trader, who sends market orders only, at a rate proportional to the gap between V and the quote it would hit and only beyond that quote: a stand-in for Glosten and Milgrom's trader and not a model of it. What it makes true by construction is not evidence about real markets (the informed fills' loss against V, VPIN's inability to lead a jump of V, the true-side VPIN's rise with any one-sided flow); the informed share is not a sufficient statistic (`kappa` raises it and lowers the loss per informed fill, `sigma_v` raises both), so a share means something only with the dial that produced it; and the same seed gives the same normals for V but not the same path once `kappa` or `persistence` changes. V is not on a recording's tape, so Reveal draws nothing in a playback and Exit playback starts V at the mid. At the dashboard's mid of 100 the floor is reached in some runs of 12,000 units.
- Notebook 14 (3U) has one simulator, five models at one set of parameters (the dashboard's) and one initial mid, so its seeds hold out the settings and not the simulator; its comparison with R-001 is of a simulator's reversal rate and not of Nasdaq's, and says nothing of whether 55.1% survives a reversal baseline. Its settings come from small grids on seeds 1 to 8 that it does not print, its best cells are the best of lists fixed in advance, and its counts of positive seeds are not tests. Its taker uses market orders and its joiner has no signal but its pulling rule, each alone in its market, and the joiner's own quotes raise the tick VPIN it reads (0.146 against 0.129), so a threshold belongs to the market it was set in. Its BVC is the bucket-level form with the standard deviation of the last 100 buckets' changes; Easley, Lopez de Prado and O'Hara (2012), as the reviews read them, classified time bars inside a bucket with a sample-wide standard deviation, which the reviews did not check against the paper. It does not test the footprint, the tape, the triggers, volume delta as a study, market resilience's bias, or VisualHFT's Book Toxicity Index and Directional Flow Pressure, whose methods are not disclosed.
- Stage 4's market (3S.9 to 3S.11; notebook 15) leaves out more than it has. **One index of all the books**: fixed weights adding up to 1 or more (the library takes any, the view the sum, so that a lot of the index is a lot of each book), a premium that reverts with the books' half-life and has no half-life of its own, no futures, no carry and no book entering or leaving the index; the sum index is a book twelve times a book's price, whose liquidity is a choice that decides whether replication pays (3S.9); **sector indexes** are not built, and what one would save a residual trader is notebook 16's section 3 (3S.12). **Stock deals only**: the acquirer pays in its own shares at a ratio fixed at the announcement, with no cash, no rival bid and no regulator; a completed target goes on as a book of its own from the offer's value, and is neither delisted nor merged into its acquirer; **an acquirer does not move at an announcement**, where a real one often does, so `NewsAwareTrader` keeps out of a deal's target by default and of its acquirer only with `acquirers=True`; a book is in one deal at a time; completed deals lift the books' levels for good (by about the premium each, outside the factors); and **a deal's premium is in ticks whatever the price**, so near the floor a ratio is far from 1 and deals mean little (the view's level is 1,000, the library's and the notebooks' 3,000). **A trend only in the factors**: the own parts must be stationary or no relation exists, so momentum here can only be a bet on a factor (Ehsani and Linnainmaa's 2022 account), and the model has no room for momentum in the residuals (Blitz, Huij and Martens 2011; Grundy and Martin 2001) or a trend in the own parts (an integrated drift would be `loading_sd` by another name, and one that keeps them stationary would change what reversal and pairs see at the half-life of 50, which is reasoned and not simulated); at the view's level a trend above about 0.04 (0.02 in a mixed market) can reach the floor over a long run. The settings of the traders and the dials' ranges come from explorations on seeds 1 to 8 that the notebook does not print, and the features are turned on one at a time: how the three act together is checked for errors and identities (69 runs of 3,000 units, 3S.9) and not studied. No rule that trades a spread of mids earns nothing before costs here, so a control is judged by losing after costs; a `DealTrader`'s records lean toward broken deals at a run's end (3S.9); and its trade is Mitchell and Pulvino's small gains and large loss, so that a fit over a few runs would rank settings by how few breaks they met, which is why the view's Deals kind has no fit (3S.11). A fit of the index kind is built and tested to work and not to help, and the library's sample of an index's books (`legs` above 0) is not on the view. Both kinds trade market orders only, as every trader here does, each alone.
- Notebook 15's findings (3S.10) are means of eight to twelve seeds of one simulated market, and what they do not show is the last bullet of 3S.10 (the model's own limits are the Stage 4 bullet above). They are for the library's market at a level of 3,000: no table was run at the view's level of 1,000, so no figure of the notebook says what the view's market gives a trader. The summaries, hints and guide of the view that quote the notebook's figures (3S.11) each say that the notebook's level is 3,000 and the view's 1,000.
- Notebook 16's findings (3S.12) are means over 24 seeds of one simulated market (section 1), over 200 seeds of a route with no order books and no costs, which is never an edge (section 2), and over eight seeds (section 3), and what they do not show is the bullet of 3S.12 on what stays open. The test of section 1 is met (t 4.98), which puts the lead far from 0 and is not its size, at one premium and two true chances with no line through them; the lead's division into the bet and a cost of reading is true by definition, and how much of the deal part is the bet is not measured; the market on the 24 new seeds did not show section 2's gap, and no cause was found, an unusual draw of seeds at the edge of chance being the reading left; and section 3's no rests on this market's price of an index lot (2.98 book lots, which the library's scale-4 book sets) and not on indexes in general. A chooser that reads the news and re-bases a target after a completion is not built. Stage 4's sector indexes are not built either: section 3 prices one in bare books and replays a trader's orders with it.
- The notebooks filter joblib's "worker stopped" warning: its workers restart when their memory has grown 300 MB since their first task, which happens after a few large simulations. No work is lost.

## 8. Recommendations & Future Enhancements

1. **Calibration on market data**: request LOBSTER's free samples, put them in `data/lobster/` and run notebook 08's last cell (`calibrate_lobster`, with a burn-in of at least its `last_unknown`); then offer the result as a parameter set in the dashboard.
2. **Stat-arb**: choose the number of PCA factors by a share of the variance, as Avellaneda and Lee also do (notebook 12 knows the true number); give books different half-lives and volatilities; try passive orders where costs decide the comparison; a verdict that allows for having picked the best of many; and more fits to a row in notebook 12's section 10 (it has three), with finer entries near 1.25 to 1.5 for pairs and baskets, to say how often *clearly better* is right.
3. **Concave metaorder impact**: add latent liquidity (orders held back and revealed as the price approaches, as in Tóth et al. 2011) to a model and test it with notebook 09's design.
4. **More long/short strategies**: all four stages are built (3S.6; stage 4, an index book, deals and a trend, is 3S.9 to 3S.11 with notebook 15). Trading less (3S.7) and sparse baskets (3S.8) are built; fitting the rate and the band over training runs, as notebook 12's section 10 does for the other settings, is not tested, nor is a fit over the two choosers of baskets or a fit of the index kind (built and tested to work, not to help; the Deals kind has no fit). What stage 3 leaves out (3S.8): a variance threshold on Box-Tiao's baskets and a test of whether one is a relation. What stage 4 leaves out (Section 7, 3S.9): an index half-life of its own, fixed-weights index traders with no chooser, cash and rival bids and an acquirer that moves, momentum in the residuals, and the three features together, studied and not only checked. **The Baskets default is an entry of 1 with a trade rate of 0.25**, changed at the user's word on 2026-11-11 after stage 3, since section 6 found it earns about +2,000 more than the old default (an entry of 2, no rate); Johansen is still the chooser (3S.8). It is not tested whether a fit helps from there. **What notebook 15 left open** was taken up in notebook 16 (3S.12), which leaves a chooser that reads the news and re-bases a target after a completion (not built; whether it would recover any of the gap that section 2 measures is not shown), how much of the deal part of the reader's lead is the bet, and the gap's size in the market beyond notebook 15's eight seeds. Sector indexes do not pay at this market's price of an index lot (what one would save at a cheaper price is read off the notebook's curve; whether a market could hold one at that price is not shown).
5. **Microstructure studies** (3T, 3U): run the studies on real data, LOBSTER's free samples (item 1) or a crypto feed, and set the simulator's findings (the queue imbalance's lift over the reversal rate, OFI's forecast, VPIN against the truth) beside what real books show; informed traders with limit orders or splitting, and a second latent value, to see whether the findings survive a richer informed trader; a trigger action that places an order, and a webhook; a mechanical sweep of the studies, the triggers and the informed model on their final files; and, if wanted, the Book Toxicity Index and Directional Flow Pressure, whose methods VisualHFT does not disclose.
6. **The workstation is out of use for this project** (the 64-core Threadripper that took one early run of notebook 13, see Re-running the Notebooks). The hundreds of segfaults it logged under heavy load on 2026-11-09 were RAM bit flips, which can also corrupt a result without a crash, so nothing computed there is to be trusted or reused, and since 2026-11-13 all work is done on the laptop. Every stored output is the laptop's: notebook 13 was run again there on 2026-11-09, and notebooks 14 and 15 and the timings of 3T.4 never ran anywhere else.
